v3.13.1 Stable2026-11-18T22:06:40Z

Automated release: version and notes generated from pull requests merged since 3.13.0.

Fixed

  • An HDR or BT.2020 RGB source is re-encoded with the matrix its output is tagged with (#544). The 10-bit line that keeps such a source's colour (x265 Main10 by default, --codec hevc, av1 and prores, export.py --preset youtube-hdr and youtube-av1) left the RGB-to-YUV conversion to FFmpeg. A source whose matrix tag is gbr (FFV1, PNG, qtrle and most RGB codecs) failed inside ffmpeg in every tool that re-encodes (-colorspace gbr is no encoder option); one with no matrix tag was written as BT.601 YUV tagged bt2020nc, so a player that follows the tag showed C03028 as C53525 (up to 15 of 255 off on FFmpeg 6.1.1). The conversion now names the matrix the line tags (bt2020nc), in the single-source tools, join.py, multicam.py, broll.py and overlay.py --video. YUV sources and SDR RGB sources are unchanged, and so are the 8-bit BT.709 lines given such a source (export.py's fixed presets, grid.py) and the alpha lines. Measured on FFmpeg 6.1.1 only.
  • fix: align HDR RGB conversion with its matrix tag (#560)

Closes: #544

What's Changed

  • fix: an HDR or BT.2020 RGB source is made YUV with the matrix its line tags by @kajisho5 in https://github.com/kajisho5/ffmpeg-skill/pull/560

Full Changelog: https://github.com/kajisho5/ffmpeg-skill/compare/v3.13.0...v3.13.1

View release →

thedotmack/claude-mem ↗ 1 release · 1 stable
v16.0.1 Stable2026-11-19T11:43:39Z

Critical fixes

  • Signing in to Codex works again on Windows while claude-mem runs (#6332, thanks @ffe111, closes #6331). The private Codex home that claude-mem's app-server uses got its auth.json through a hard link to your real ~/.codex/auth.json. Codex refuses to replace a credential file with more than one hard link (persist_failed: atomic credential replacement does not support hard-linked files), so codex login and token refresh failed for as long as that home existed. The private home now gets a symlink, which Codex follows. Only on Windows, and only when a symlink is not permitted (no Developer Mode or elevation), it gets a private copy instead. Any other error still fails loudly.
  • A probe answered with <skip_summary/> ends the quota cooldown (#6324, thanks @vaibhav375, closes #6322). After a quota hit, the worker probes the provider and clears the cooldown once a probe stores a memory. A probe the provider answered with a valid <skip_summary/> stores nothing, so the cooldown never cleared and the worker re-probed every 5 minutes for as long as that session only skipped. A valid skip now releases the cooldown too, on both the store path and the early path before the memory session id is known. Observer-health success still needs a real store.

Fixes

  • Search falls back to SQLite instead of failing when Chroma is slow (#6327, thanks @REI-330, closes #6323). The MCP client gives a search 30 s, while each Chroma read waited up to 60 s, so a slow query was still running when the caller gave up and the SQLite/FTS5 fallback never ran. A search now shares one 20 s budget across its Chroma reads and falls back when it runs out, without restarting a healthy chroma-mcp.
  • Chroma rebuilds no longer hold a core for hours (#6299, thanks @srieteja). A full backfill now pauses after each row for its write time times CLAUDE_MEM_CHROMA_BACKFILL_PAUSE_RATIO (default 1, 0 turns it off), and chroma-mcp starts under nice (CLAUDE_MEM_CHROMA_PROCESS_NICE, default 10) outside Windows. Live sync is unchanged.
  • Windows process cleanup reads taskkill correctly (#6328, thanks @REI-330; #6307). A taskkill exit 255 is now judged by whether the named processes are still alive, not by its wording, so a localized message no longer throws during Chroma teardown, and a mixed message no longer reports success while a process still runs.
  • Observer turns stop leaking SQLite statements (#6306). The statements that store an observer turn are cached instead of prepared on every turn, which could pile up until SQLite ran out of memory and rolled back the whole turn.
  • The login notice says when the observer has no Claude Code login it can use (#6321, refs #6319). Instead of telling you to run /login when the terminal is signed in to another credential profile, the worker logs a warning with the reason and the notice names which login it read and suggests claude setup-token.
  • The legacy claude-mem shell alias is found and removed on install and update (#6300, thanks @0xABCD01). doctor reports it, status warns when the worker runs outside the resolved plugin root, multiline aliases are left untouched with a warning, and nothing is changed if you cancel the reinstall.
  • /api/health publishes probe state only (#6309, thanks @DeryFerd). It no longer shows the auth method, the Redis queue descriptor, absolute install paths or the last interaction to unauthenticated callers.
  • An unreadable My Work refresh status no longer breaks setup (#6305). A damaged refresh.json reads as no run and gets replaced; the auto-updater still treats it as busy.
  • Local work stays paused when the machine runs out of file descriptors (#6303). The setup check treats EMFILE/ENFILE as "setup still active" and logs one warning per episode.
  • Timeline file labels look the same on every platform (#6301, thanks @POWDER-RANGER).
  • A press anywhere on a My Work focus card opens its area (#6315). The same fix covers the List, Team and By agent rows.

New

  • Devin cloud sessions and Devin Bots (#6334). An organization can install plugin/ as a Devin plugin and get hooks, MCP search and skills in every session.
  • My Work refresh on OpenCode (#6330, thanks @REI-330). Opt in with CLAUDE_MEM_WORK_VIEW_AGENT=opencode; nothing changes otherwise.
  • PostgreSQL search finds terms inside CJK, kana and Hangul text (#6329, thanks @REI-330, refs #3982).
  • My Work card titles name the task in 5 to 7 words instead of copying the first sentence of the ask (#6317).
  • The image viewer darkens and blurs the page behind it and casts a shadow (#6313).
  • The installer asks for your work email and a message in the terminal before GUI setup (#6308).

Tests and docs

  • Grok Bot and Zed tests pass on Windows (#6311, #6312, thanks @Lupin133).
  • Community sweep worktrees install with bun install (#6325).

nexu-io/open-design ↗ 1 release · 1 stable
open-design-v0.27.2Open Design 0.27.2 — Clearer Quotas, Sharper Displays Stable2026-11-19T10:59:48Z

🌟 Codename: Clearer Quotas, Sharper Displays

0.27.2 is a focused reliability patch for the 0.27 line. No new surface—just clearer signals when a limit resets, a project action fails, a display changes, or a Mac app updates itself.

🐛 Fixed

🖥️ Desktop and presentation

  • Mac windows recover their sharpness after a display change. A wake or monitor move no longer has to leave the app stretched at the wrong scale; refocusing gives the renderer another chance to match the display it is on. (#9409)
  • Installed macOS updates have a steadier handoff. Payload updates now run through the installed app’s lifecycle, so a failed generation can recover without trapping the next launch on a broken handoff. (#8936) Thanks @PerishCode.

💳 Workspace and billing

  • Quota reset times keep the minutes that matter. When a reset is days away, the quota panel now still tells you the remaining minutes instead of making the deadline feel less precise than it is. (#9328)
  • A failed move to team space now looks like a failure. Project-card menu errors use the same clear error treatment as the rest of the workspace, so the next action is easier to judge. (#9409)

📥 Download: Tag open-design-v0.27.2.

🙏 Thanks to everyone who shipped 0.27.2

@PerishCode · @lefarcen · @alchemistklk

stablyai/orca ↗ 1 release · 1 stable
v1.4.264 Stable2026-11-19T10:35:30Z

Previous release: 1.4.263 — Lighter chat startup, Hermes in Chat and browser extension side panels.

Chat commands find their workspace ✨

This desktop patch corrects workspace selection when a Chat UI agent runs Orca commands from a folder outside its workspace.

  • 💬 Keep browser work with the chat: When Orca can locate the chat, commands such as opening a browser tab use that chat's workspace, including folder workspaces, rather than the workspace currently in focus.

Usage notes

If a chat's workspace cannot be resolved, browser commands without a target are refused. Run the command from the intended workspace folder or pass --worktree; commands acting on an existing page also accept --page using an ID from orca tab list --json.

What's Changed

🧰 CLI & automation

Resolve the chat's workspace for commands run outside it, and require an explicit browser target when resolution fails.

  • Target the chat's own workspace when its agent runs outside it by @helldweller in https://github.com/stablyai/orca/pull/44336
  • Refuse unscoped browser commands from chat agents outside a workspace by @brennanb2025 in https://github.com/stablyai/orca/pull/44237

New Contributors

  • @helldweller made their first contribution in https://github.com/stablyai/orca/pull/44336

Full Changelog: https://github.com/stablyai/orca/compare/v1.4.263...v1.4.264

react@0.2.0@openviking/react 0.2.0 Stable2026-11-19T05:11:24Z

en-US

Minor Changes

  • 609baba: Let FileBrowser hosts load experience metrics and override individual experience dashboard sections.

  • 704bec6: Add optional FileBrowser props for hosts to save and restore expanded folders independently of the selected resource.

    Hosts can also persist explicit collapses across mounts when home alias resolution is temporarily unavailable.

Patch Changes

  • 7a15a83: Make the AgentConnect full-instruction copy button more prominent and clarify its labels. Allow the instruction header and copy button to wrap in narrow containers regardless of component stylesheet import order. Unify rounded, theme-aware instruction cards, code blocks and tables, and keep a single table border even when only a header is present.

  • 9dd10ec: Consolidate compile help under one Console entry and document the model and schedule fields in --args.

  • 8a52885: Support session auto-creation, memory policies, context token budgets, and retained message counts in Console. Send structured message parts, creation timestamps, and batch JSON. Session write commands accept boolean or JSON object telemetry options and display the server's telemetry summary with a label in the current Console language.

  • 4c11e48: Console can restore read-only command history after a refresh in the same browser tab when the host provides a scoped commandHistoryKey.

  • 9f03f45: Add Compile create before Compile in the Console quick-start actions and fill in the command syntax for both. Clarify the compile configuration and directory parameters in the built-in help, and keep parameter descriptions readable in narrow popovers.

  • 989e6c7: Show the error message directly when a command fails. If the API error message is blank, show its error code.

  • 9f03f45: Keep Console command history in local storage for each configured library and user scope. The history menu and arrow-key recall restore the same submitted commands after a reload, including asynchronous resource imports.

  • 61a0546: Keep resource URIs with spaces clickable in Console result URI fields and quoted URI arguments. In plain text, Markdown code, and free-text command arguments, link each URI token separately.

  • 8a52885: Keep the Deleted badge on confirmed file deletions in the history and comparison picker. The file must exist in the first parent. Older servers can confirm deletion through snapshot diff. Compare with an earlier version to see the removed content. Missing snapshot content for a tracked file appears as a read error.

  • 8a52885: Disable dates outside the selected search range and reject reversed ranges entered by hand.

  • 442638c: Restore compile create as a separate Console entry before compile, split their guidance, and align creation parameters with the latest product copy.

  • 94b18d3: Switch retrieval modes at the top of the FileBrowser results area, including when the result count is zero. Switching modes retains the query and compatible filters.

  • 8a52885: Show the resource name on the preview tab immediately when opening a Wiki compilation or conflict dashboard.

  • c16c4cc: VikingBot now uses dedicated controls for Wiki and scheduled update questions, supports submitting the current session for memory extraction, and presents rename and delete in a menu on each history entry.

    Console can also show a Bot activation screen and open the conversation when a host confirms the current library's Bot is ready.

  • 704bec6: Return focus to the VikingBot session history button when a session dialog closes and focus would otherwise fall back to the page. The tooltip stays hidden after mouse dismissal and remains available on hover and keyboard focus.

  • 8a52885: Keep Wiki conflict highlights visible while any part of a highlighted block remains. Open the selected conflict in the editor, close conflict tooltips when scrolling or leaving the editor, and show a success notice after saving a Wiki file.

zh-CN

功能更新

  • 609baba: 支持 FileBrowser 宿主加载经验应用指标，并按区块覆盖经验看板的展示。

  • 704bec6: FileBrowser 增加可选属性，支持宿主独立保存和恢复目录展开状态与选中资源。

    主目录别名暂时无法解析时，宿主也可通过完整快照跨挂载保存手动折叠记录。

  • 7a15a83: 突出 AgentConnect 完整指令复制按钮并明确其文案，支持说明标题栏和复制按钮在窄容器内换行，不受组件样式引入顺序影响。统一说明卡片、代码块和表格的圆角及主题背景，仅有表头的表格也保持单层边框。

Imbad0202/academic-research-skills ↗ 1 release · 1 stable
v3.24.4v3.24.4 — A committee-correspondence variant scoped to research ethics review, below-consensus findings in the roadmap and letter, and GPT-6.2 Astra as the recommended OpenAI verifier Stable2026-11-19T09:46:06Z

Reviewer, revision-coach, cross-model, and Windows repairs; no new skill or mode, and the prompt-level changes are unmeasured: The revision-coach committee-correspondence variant now covers only comments from a formal research ethics or human-subjects protection review of research with human participants, their data, or their biological samples, made by a body with formal review authority over the study. Before it takes the variant, the coach starts its reply by quoting the user's own words that name the body; when the user's turns do not name it, the coach asks which body sent the comments and in what review (#907). The preregistered eval runs used an earlier text of these rules, and the final rules have not been run in a plugin eval; a retest is tracked in #1122. Known limits of the gate, such as a named body whose kind of review is unknown, are listed in #1123. The letter gains a Below the Consensus Bar section for round-1 findings that two of the four non-DA reviewers raised with no conflict, or that one raised, and revision-roadmap/1.1 gives them the levels CORROBORATED and SINGLE-REVIEWER; a revision-roadmap/1.0 roadmap stays readable but cannot carry these values (#1100). The Devil's Advocate files an unsupported premise that a core claim depends on as an issue row, and the synthesizer marks a single-reviewer finding when a DA issue row reports it too; the mark does not raise the finding's level and is not evidence of independent judgement (#1098). The reviewer_full contract digest changes, so seeded-defect runs under the old digest are not directly comparable. The field analyst names the reviewer seat that checks who the manuscript assumes will benefit and who bears the cost, or writes Seat: none and states the gap (#1098). The domain reviewer judges literature coverage as of the date the manuscript states; the CHANGELOG entry gives its rules for a manuscript with no date and for works that may postdate it (part of #1107). GPT-6.2 Astra is a provisional cross-model verifier and the recommended OpenAI id; it passed the entry-gate smoke on the ChatGPT-subscription citation transport only, no Promotion Bakeoff is recorded, and the GPT-6.2 system card has not been audited for ARS. On the Responses API route, openai_effort_guard.sh rejects the efforts none, minimal, and ultra for it before a request leaves. Five file readers that do not follow symlinks no longer fail outright on Windows, and the inquiry branch ledger skips its directory fsync where a directory cannot be opened, so a crash right after a rename on Windows can lose the new directory entry (#1092). The Windows path is best-effort and not atomic, does not check every directory on the path (the CHANGELOG entry lists which it trusts), refuses every reparse point it checks (cloud-sync placeholders included), and was tested only by simulation on other hosts: no Windows run was made. The inquiry branch ledger alpha still refuses to run on Windows, and a binding manifest whose reference contains a colon no longer passes validation on any platform. For Chicago, MLA, IEEE, and Vancouver papers, citation_compliance_agent reports findings and auto-corrects nothing until a full fix lands, and some findings can still be wrong (#1093). Each way the academic-paper full revision loop ends now has one next step (#1087), the revision token check no longer stalls on a long run of capitals or digits (#1088), and MLA 9th conversion keeps a DOI that the entry has (#1095). Cross-model docs and comments, and the verifier prompt's NOT_FOUND definition, now say what the checks show (#1111, #1113, #1114, #1116), and the reviewer examples show the Coverage and Stakeholder Coverage rules (#1085).

Changed

  • The revision-coach committee-correspondence variant covers only a formal research ethics or human-subjects protection review of the study, and the coach quotes the user's words that name the body (#907). The variant's tracker ends with a fixed human-subjects boundary, but Routing Rule 7 sent any explicitly named real committee or institutional review office to it. It now covers a letter or comments from a research ethics or human-subjects protection review of research with human participants, their data, or their biological samples, made by a body with formal review authority over the study, such as a research ethics committee, an institutional review board (IRB), or a human research ethics office. The kind of review decides, not the topic of each comment, so such a review's comments on statistics, data access, or safety monitoring stay in the variant, and so does a formal review that such a body holds to decide whether the study needs its review; a mock or advisory review before submission does not count. A review held for another purpose (scientific merit, statistics, data access, or safety monitoring), a thesis or dissertation committee, and a grant or funding panel take normal revision-coach. Before taking the branch, the coach starts its reply with the user's own words that name the body, in quotation marks; when the user's own turns do not name it, the coach asks which body sent the comments and in what review, and a yes or no reply does not count. The full scope and quote rules are stated once, in the Activation gate of committee_correspondence_protocol.md. .claude/CLAUDE.md, academic-paper/SKILL.md, revision_coach_agent.md, mode_selection_guide.md, and commands/ars-revision-coach.md load the protocol when the user says, or the pasted text shows, that the comments may come from such a review, and say that the gate decides and that comments it excludes take normal revision-coach; revision_coach_agent.md now also loads it for resubmission organization, which the gate already accepted. scripts/test_check_committee_correspondence.py pins the rules to the gate section and the pointer to the first four files. The #900 guard sentence and the variant's outputs do not change. Plugin-eval case 03 gains a with-only llm grader, no-false-committee-claim, that fails an answer saying the user named a committee; judged once by sonnet, its final text failed the one known misroute among 14 stored case-03 answers, passed the other 13, and judged 9 constructed test answers as expected (seven of them were added after review or after an earlier text misjudged one). New case 08 sends the case-04 letter with the mode command. In preregistered runs on Claude Opus 5.5 (plugin-evals/preregistration-907.md, plugin-evals/preregistration-907-command.md), case 03 passed 3 of 3 and case 04 fired the skill in 0 of 3, so it did not test the change; in case 08 the one readable run took the variant but did not quote the user's words. Those runs used an earlier text of the scope and quote rules and of the grader; the final text has not been run, and a retest of case 08 is tracked in #1122. Known limits of the gate and its tests (an informal consultation after submission, a named body whose kind of review is unknown, an overlap in the grader's program-committee exemption, and a copy check that skips the command file) are listed in #1123. Prompt-level.

  • GPT-6.2 Astra is a provisional cross-model verifier and the recommended OpenAI id. OpenAI released gpt-6.2-astra on 2026-11-17. It passed the entry-gate smoke on the ChatGPT-subscription citation transport on 2026-11-18 (scripts/cross_model_smoke_test_codex.sh, codex-cli 0.170.0: VERIFIED with a bound source); no API-route smoke and no Promotion Bakeoff is recorded, so it is provisional on both transports. The recommendation moves to it under the #783 lifecycle policy, which makes no measurement claim. gpt-6-astra stays listed as provisional, with no shutdown date announced. shared/cross_model_verification.md, the id-status allowlist, docs/SETUP.md and docs/SETUP.zh-TW.md, and .claude/CLAUDE.md change together. OpenAI's model catalog lists the same reasoning-effort values for GPT-6.2 Astra as for GPT-6 Astra (low|medium|high|xhigh|max), so openai_effort_guard.sh applies the GPT-6 Astra row to both ids and, on the Responses API route, rejects none, minimal, and ultra before a request leaves. The subscription transport keeps its own closed effort set, which is not model-specific. The internal audit scripts that pin gpt-6-astra (run_codex_audit.sh, dispatch_panel_claim_audit.py) keep it, because the part-one #1002 held-out audit used it as a judge, and that result does not carry over to another model. The recommendation sentence for a validated OpenAI id now says which route each id is validated on, and docs/RISK_REGISTER.md R6 names GPT-6 Astra as the model its system-card citation covers. The GPT-6.2 system card has not been audited for ARS.

  • The domain reviewer judges literature coverage as of the manuscript's own date, so a work published later is not reported as missed (part of #1107). domain_reviewer_agent.md asked whether "key developments from the last 3-5 years" were covered, with no date to count from, so a work that appeared after the manuscript was written could be listed as a missing reference. The reviewer now writes the manuscript's reference date (the date it states for the version under review, the latest one when it states several, and where it is stated), counts the 3-5 years back from it, and names a later work only under a new #### Later Work the Authors May Want to Note heading, which is not a weakness and changes no score. With no stated date, the reference date is unknown and coverage is judged as of the review date, with a "may postdate the manuscript" note on works from the 12 months before it; with any date, a work the reviewer cannot place before or after the coverage date is listed with the same note; the year of the latest cited work is not used, because a manuscript whose citations stop years ago would then never be told that it missed recent work. Prompt-level; the effect on reviews is unmeasured. The opt-in retrieval step that #1107 also proposes is not part of this change.

  • Findings below the consensus bar get a roadmap level and a place in the letter; the field analyst names the seat that checks who benefits; D3 names an unsupported premise (#1100, #1098). revision-roadmap/1.1 adds CORROBORATED (two of the four non-DA seats, no conflict) and SINGLE-REVIEWER (one seat) to consensus_level, which had no value for these round-1 findings; producers emit 1.1, and a revision-roadmap/1.0 roadmap stays readable but cannot carry the new values. Synthesizer Step 5 maps each Step 2 label to its level, keeps SINGLE-VERIFIER for re-review previously_missed items, and copies a matching DA issue row into corroborating_sources[] with reviewer: DA; the DA row never changes agree, the level, or the obligation class. The Editorial Decision Letter gains a Below the Consensus Bar section with the DA-corroborated note, so the label from #1098 now reaches the author. field_analyst_agent.md writes a ## Stakeholder Coverage section that names the card (#2-#4) whose identity can check who the manuscript assumes will benefit, who bears the cost, and who is left out, with a neutral Review Focus item, or writes Seat: none and states the gap; the user sees it with the cards before Phase 1. The reviewer_full contract's D3 (argumentative coherence, mandatory, scored by the DA and the methodology seat, owned by the DA) now names an unsupported premise that a core claim depends on, so a D3 block on such a premise leads to a major revision; the contract SHA-256 changes, the panel provenance pins follow, and evals/heldout/reviewer_seeded_defects/README.md records that runs under the old digest are not directly comparable. Prompt-level; unmeasured. A new seeded-defect baseline is not part of this change.

  • The Devil's Advocate files a premise the argument depends on as an issue row, and the synthesizer marks a single-reviewer finding that the DA also reports (#1098). A premise-level critique, such as an assumption about who benefits, who bears the cost, or who is left out, could reach the synthesizer only as one seat's finding: the DA wrote it in its Missing Stakeholder Perspectives list or the optional Unexamined Premise section, which are not part of its reviewer_full Review Body grammar. The DA Phase 2 prompt that reviewer_full dispatches (item 3, in devils_advocate_reviewer_agent.md and its canonical source references/reviewer_sprint_prompt_source.md, re-pinned) now says that a premise, stated or unstated, that a core claim depends on and that the paper does not support must be a CRITICAL or MAJOR row with a typed Evidence Anchor, banded by its decision impact, and that mentioning it only in prose or in a list or section does not satisfy the rule. Challenge Dimension 7, Frame-Lock Detection, and the DA report structure in SKILL.md carry the same sentence, and Dimension 7 and Frame-Lock point to Phase 2 item 3 for reviewer_full. In editorial_synthesizer_agent.md, a DA issue row that reports a sub-claim a scoring seat raised is recorded in the Step 1b inventory and never counted; a single-reviewer finding with such a row is labelled single-reviewer finding, DA-corroborated, agree stays 1 over the four non-DA reviewers, both severities are shown, and no difference from a DA row makes a scoring seat disputed. The label is not evidence of independent judgement. Limitation: in this change the label exists in the synthesizer's Step 2 only; the #1100 entry above carries it into the roadmap and the letter. examples/subclaim_decomposition_example.md adds a synthetic case. Seat assignment for stakeholder coverage and the D3 premise wording are in the #1100 entry above. Prompt-level; unmeasured.

Fixed

  • The MLA 9th part of citation_format_switcher.md no longer says to remove DOIs (#1095). Its MLA key rules said "no DOI in basic format (include if online)", and step 4 of the APA → MLA steps said "Remove DOIs (unless online-only source)", so a literal reading removed a DOI from an MLA entry for a journal article that also exists in print. Both lines now say to keep each DOI that the entry has and to carry over a DOI that the supplied source data gives, without requiring a lookup for a missing one, and the MLA Works Cited example now ends with a DOI. This is a new conversion instruction for ARS, not a statement of the MLA Handbook; it agrees with the MLA 9th cell of the DOI row in citation_compliance_agent.md, which says only "Optional".

  • The two academic-paper-reviewer examples show the Coverage, card-location, and Stakeholder Coverage rules, and the Reviewer 3 Quality Gate allows a practical-application angle (#1085). examples/hei_paper_review_example.md and examples/interdisciplinary_review_example.md went from the Target Journal section straight to the cards, with no Coverage section. Each now has a Coverage table (#1075) and a Stakeholder Coverage block (#1098). Each existing Review Focus line and each "Will particularly care about" line keeps its wording and now ends with the manuscript locations it applies to (#1076); the two lines that stated a conclusion as a fact ("no visible feature selection strategy", "Absence of organizational decline theory") now ask the question instead. HEI Card #3 and interdisciplinary Card #4 each gain a fourth Review Focus item, the stakeholder check that the Stakeholder Coverage block assigns to that seat. The cards were ### under an empty #### heading; Phase 0 now holds the report sections at ###, with the cards at #### under ### Reviewer Configuration Cards, which stays within the #{1,4} card headings that scripts/dispatch_e4_panel.py reads. In the HEI example, Reviewer 1's question about the interview guide now agrees with the Coverage row that marks the appendix as read. The examples still have no Review Strategy Recommendations section. The Quality Gate in field_analyst_agent.md asked Reviewer 3 for "a specific different disciplinary perspective", although Configuration Principle 4 also allows a practical-application angle; the gate now accepts either. Example text and one prompt line; unmeasured.

  • Cross-model verification text no longer reads more into the search signal, the uri check, a NOT_FOUND, or a NOT_SEARCHED than the checks show (#1116). The header of scripts/cross_model_verification/gemini_is_grounded.jq and the Gemini block of shared/cross_model_verification.md said the guard requires that the model "actually issued a search" (the doc: "the model issued a search"); the filter checks only that webSearchQueries is a non-empty array, so [null] meets it, and both now say that the response reports a search and that its elements are not checked. They also said the extraction yields at least one url; it keeps any non-empty string uri, a whitespace-only one included, and both now say so. The Sampling parameters note said the grounding guards "enforce an actual lookup"; it now says they decide whether a response's search evidence is accepted. The verification prompt in Step 3 and in scripts/cross_model_smoke_test.sh defined NOT_FOUND as "searched, no matching record exists"; both now say "no matching record was found", which is what a search that finds nothing supports. This changes the text the verifier model receives. The Step 6 report line explained a NOT_SEARCHED count as "the cross-model could not actually search", which attributes every NOT_SEARCHED to an inability to search, although a reference can also reach it by a downgrade; it now says the verdict was NOT_SEARCHED or was downgraded to it, and lists the four ways a reference gets there (the model said it could not search, a VERIFIED came with no source, the grounding check failed, or a compatible provider's positive verdict was downgraded). The headers of gemini_is_grounded.jq and gemini_sources.jq said a non-object at a dereference point would crash jq; a null does not, and they now say a non-object can make jq fail. The guard and extraction logic do not change.

  • The Gemini invalid-index note and the one-call-per-reference rationale no longer claim more than the code shows (#1114). shared/cross_model_verification.md (Gemini block) and the header of scripts/cross_model_verification/gemini_is_grounded.jq said that an invalid index, the wrong candidate, or a non-string uri "all leave the extraction blank" and fail the guard closed. Each chunk index is checked on its own, so a response whose first support has an out-of-range index and whose second has a valid one (an index whose chunk has a non-empty string uri) still meets the guard's source condition; the guard also needs a non-empty webSearchQueries. Both now say that such an index contributes no source and that the source condition fails only when no index yields a source. The .jq header also lists malformed values that empty the whole extraction, and says only that a NOT_FOUND or MISMATCH with no source link is never trusted. The header of gemini_sources.jq said that a malformed support set yields blank sources; it now says the same as the guard. Step 3 said a single grounding trace on a batched response "proves something was searched", and the Cost Considerations note called one call per reference "the only way" to map grounding evidence 1:1 to each verdict; both now give the narrower reason, that with one reference per call each call's grounding evidence belongs to one reference and its verdict. Comments and documentation only; the guard logic does not change.

  • The cross-model cost notes and grounding-guard comments say what the code shows (#1113). In shared/cross_model_verification.md, the risk-stratification note said cost scales with the high-impact count "instead of total reference count"; for a fixed high-impact count the total can still change the sampled tier (with 12 high-impact references, 42 references give 15 calls at Stage 2.5 and 60 give 17), and the note now says so. The note and the integrity cost row said a "results-dense" paper approaches full coverage; they now say that coverage approaches 100% as the high-impact share of the references does. The Cost Considerations section now says that its dollar figures assume citation calls use the first-party API, and that with ARS_CROSS_MODEL_TRANSPORT=codex citation calls draw on the ChatGPT subscription while the DA and checkpoint calls still use the API, and the integrity cost row states the sampling rule in full (10% of the remainder, rounded up, minimum 3, maximum 10, all of it when fewer than 3). Several comments described a failed grounding guard as a known fact about the search; a malformed body or a filter error fails the same check. The OpenAI and Gemini failure-branch comments, the Gemini grounding note (now limited to a 2xx response), the HTTP-error comment (which now says only that relabelling would hide an HTTP or network error), and the headers of scripts/cross_model_verification/openai_has_completed_web_search.jq and gemini_is_grounded.jq now say what the guard can or cannot verify, and the CI note says that a regression in the tested cases fails CI rather than that the guard cannot stop failing closed. Comments and documentation only; the guard logic does not change.

  • Four stale statements in the cross-model verification docs are corrected (#1111). docs/SETUP.md, docs/SETUP.zh-TW.md, and shared/cross_model_verification.md named the codex citation request codex_citation_request/1.0; the transport accepts only ars-codex-citation-request/1.0, so a request built from that comment exited 2. The model-detection block in shared/cross_model_verification.md said that without jq the API calls would use a python3 fallback; there is none, so the warning now says that API-route calls fail until jq is installed, and it is shown only for the api transport, which is the one that calls jq. The Promotion Bakeoff entry gate now names the smoke script for each transport: scripts/cross_model_smoke_test.sh on the first-party API route and scripts/cross_model_smoke_test_codex.sh on the ChatGPT-subscription citation transport, which the #787 run used. The API-route script accepts gpt-* ids only, so a Gemini candidate still has no entry gate. The Cost Considerations worked example counted the reference selection at one integrity gate; Stage 2.5 and Stage 4.5 each run it, so the example is now 34 citation calls when no non-high-impact reference supports a new or changed claim, and about 39-40 calls for the full pipeline (was about 22-23); the table also says that each such reference adds a Stage 4.5 NEW-CHANGED call and reduces the pool from which CONTROL is sampled, and the estimate is about $3.20-7.00 (was about $1.85-4.05), at the same per-call rates. Documentation only; no script changes.

  • The no-follow file readers and the ledger's directory fsync no longer fail outright on Windows, and review_criteria_binding.py no longer ends in a raw traceback when it reads a file (#1092). Six scripts opened a directory with os.open() and five then walked the path with dir_fd=: revision_roadmap.py (ArtifactStore._open_relative), review_criteria_binding.py, adjudication_activity.py, run_review_criteria_constructive_value.py, score_review_criteria_constructive_value.py, and, for the directory open alone, the fsync in inquiry_branch_ledger.py. On Windows os.open() cannot open a directory and os.open is not in os.supports_dir_fd, so each failed before reading anything, with an error that looked like a problem with the user's file. In review_criteria_binding.py the error was raised outside the except BindingError, so the user saw a traceback. New scripts/_anchored_open.py owns the walk and the fsync. On POSIX it keeps the descriptor-relative walk (O_DIRECTORY | O_NOFOLLOW per component, leaf O_NOFOLLOW) unchanged. Where os.open is not in os.supports_dir_fd or os.name is nt, it walks by path instead: each component is lstat-ed and a symlink or any reparse point (a junction is one) is refused, the leaf is opened by path, its identity is compared with the lstat, and every component is lstat-ed again after the open. Limits, stated in the module: this is best-effort and has no Windows CI job; it is not atomic, so a component swapped and restored between the checks goes undetected, where the POSIX walk closes that race; the anchor's own ancestors and a relative path's current directory are trusted, and only the anchor itself is checked when a caller asks for that (the two artifact-store readers do); and every reparse point is refused, cloud-sync placeholders included. A rejection there says it is a symlink or reparse point found by a path-based, best-effort check (the adjudication store reports it as a symlink or reparse point, not only a symlink), and a platform that lacks what a safe open needs raises PlatformLimitation. The helper refuses a path component that has a separator, a drive prefix, or (on Windows) a colon. The binding and scorer readers now turn any OSError or NotImplementedError from the open into their own error type. All five readers also turn a ValueError from the open (the helper refusing such a component, or an embedded NUL) into their own error type, so it no longer ends in a traceback. review_criteria_binding.py now refuses a colon anywhere in a portable reference (context_ref, registry_ref, artifact_ref, and --artifact ROLE=path), on every platform, and the manifest schema's portable_ref pattern refuses it too: on Windows C:/x is absolute, C:x is relative to that drive's current directory, and name:stream names an alternate data stream, and the old check let all three through. A binding manifest whose reference contains a colon no longer validates. Not covered: Windows reserved device names (CON, NUL) and names ending in a dot or a space are still accepted as references. The directory fsync is skipped where a directory cannot be opened, so a crash right after a rename on Windows can lose the new directory entry; the inquiry branch ledger alpha still refuses to run on Windows because of its lock backend (#845). suite_lock.json is re-sealed for the two measurement scripts, as in #713; no measurement run is changed. scripts/test_anchored_open.py (CI manifest id 1092-anchored-open) runs both backends on any host, and simulates Windows with an empty os.supports_dir_fd and an os.open that rejects dir_fd and directories; it also covers a reparse point with no symlink mode, a leaf swapped before the open, and a parent swapped for a symlink after it. Not in this change: pdf_read_preflight.py --output and build_submission_packet_manifest.py still refuse non-POSIX hosts by design, and pipeline_orchestrator_agent.md still tells every platform to pass --output; build_review_pathway_rule_trace.py still calls os.fchmod, which Python 3.12 lacks on Windows; run_ideation_diversity_no_call.py keeps its own directory fsync; review_criteria_binding.py still calls os.fchmod when it records a receipt (_atomic_replace), and Windows has no os.fchmod before Python 3.13, so that write can still end in a traceback there; record --manifest . still ends in a raw ValueError, because the lock-file name is built outside the error handling. No Windows run was made.

  • Reviewer prompt drift: one lint for the #1098 sentence, source order in the sub-claim example, and one DA dimension count (#1099, #1101). scripts/check_1098_premise_rule_sync.py reads the #1098 premise-row sentence from the da-phase2 fragment of references/reviewer_sprint_prompt_source.md and fails unless the canonical sentence sits on one line, the DA agent file holds one exact copy in each of Phase 2, Challenge Dimension 7, and Frame-Lock Detection, the canonical source holds 1 and the reviewer SKILL.md 1, and every (#1098). ending closes an exact copy; scripts/test_check_1098_premise_rule_sync.py breaks, removes, adds, and moves copies one at a time, and runs through the CI pytest manifest. examples/subclaim_decomposition_example.md drops its "Priority 1" and "Priority 2" headings and shows the two roadmap items in source order with their obligation class, as Step 5 of the synthesizer requires. devils_advocate_reviewer_agent.md states its dimensions as 8 challenges and 1 severity calibration: dimensions 1 to 8 challenge the paper and dimension 9 calibrates the DA's own findings, so Frame-Lock Detection keeps "8 challenge dimensions"; the DA detection hash is re-pinned. Prompt-level; unmeasured.

  • citation_compliance_agent no longer auto-corrects citations or references in Chicago, MLA, IEEE, or Vancouver papers (#1093, interim). Several of its checks apply APA 7th forms to every style: the DOI/URL Verification checklist checks every DOI against https://doi.org/ and asks for no period after it, and the Core Verification Rules by Format table calls a DOI required in IEEE and Vancouver. Until the full fix lands, a new rule at the top of the file says that for these four styles the agent auto-corrects nothing in any mode: it runs no correction, conversion, sorting, or rebuilding step, proposes no corrections in revision mode, lists no corrected reference list or replacement citations in citation-check mode, where a corrected version of the user's text still comes only on request and begins with a note that it may be wrong (academic-paper/SKILL.md says the same), reports each finding for review, and passes the draft and reference list on unchanged. Some of these findings can still be wrong, because the checks themselves were written for APA 7th; the report says so. APA 7th behavior does not change. The full fix is in draft PR #1096. Prompt-level; unmeasured.

  • The revision token-conservation check no longer stalls on a long run of capital letters or digits (#1088). In scripts/check_revision_token_conservation.py, the narrative-author part of AUTHOR_YEAR_RE could split one capitalized word into many shorter words, so a run of capitals with no year after it backtracked exponentially: 32 capitals took 0.75 s, and each 2 more took about 2.6 times longer. Each author word now has to end where the word ends, and the author phrase does not start right after a capital letter, whose match would start at that capital anyway. On "A" * n + " x", 50,000 capitals now take about 2 ms. A single word of 10,000 letters in alternating case still takes about 0.3 s, because the scan restarts at each capital that follows a small letter. The tokens do not change: over 300,000 random strings and the 201,916 Markdown lines of the repository that are at most 3,000 characters long and contain no match for [A-Z\w'’-]{22,} under Python's default Unicode matching (such runs can stall the old pattern), the old and new patterns found the same matches at the same positions. SQUARE_CITATION_RE had the same fault with digits: [ and a run of digits with no ] after it backtracked exponentially (22 digits took 0.14 s, and each 2 more took about 4 times longer). It now reads a digit and then any digits and separators up to ], and 200,000 digits take about 1 ms. Over 300,000 random strings and the 218,605 Markdown lines of the repository that hold no run of 18 or more digits, the old and new patterns found the same matches. An author chain with an unclosed parenthesis full of years, such as 400 Ab and and then ( and 400 2020 , still takes about 1.5 s; that form is not fixed. The academic-pipeline orchestrator runs this check once per revision round.

  • Each way the academic-paper full revision loop ends now has one next step, and three files state the same rules (#1087). formatter_agent started Phase 7 only on an Accept, but the loop could end without one: after a Round 2 Minor Revision verdict no review followed the last allowed revision, and after Round 2 the user could pick Option A. Option A also clashed with Checkpoint Rule 4, which says that a Critical issue blocks Phase 7. academic-paper/SKILL.md Checkpoint Rule 6, a new peer_reviewer_agent § Loop End States table, and a new formatter_agent § Review Status now say the same thing. Phase 7 starts without a stop on an Accept for the draft that goes to it. After the last allowed revision, after Option A, or on an early-formatting request, it starts only after the user confirms it at a stop that shows the draft's Review Status. Option C ends the workflow without Phase 7. Option A is not offered while a Critical issue remains; the user can still ask for early formatting. Under Option A, draft_writer_agent adds the "Acknowledged Limitations" section before Phase 7. The output package carries a Review Status row, and the formatter calls a draft "passed peer review" only when the line is Accepted in Round N. format-convert mode, standalone disclosure mode, and academic-pipeline Stage 5 do not change. draft_writer_agent and citation_compliance_agent name the same drafts in their handoffs to the formatter. Three related repairs: peer_reviewer_agent now reads the Revision Log in draft_writer_agent's format (author triage, then Action Taken), not as a list of "Resolved" items; it gives the formatter the Final Counts (body words, in-text citations, reference entries) only with an Accept, and otherwise the formatter counts the draft itself; citation_compliance_agent marks its Common Auto-Corrections as APA 7th rules, applies each row of its Common Citation Error Patterns only for a style that has that rule (the missing-year row only for author-date styles), and in the Step 4 DOI/URL Check flags a missing DOI only when the style requires or expects one and checks for a period after a DOI or URL only in APA 7th; its separate DOI/URL Verification checklist and Step 4's DOI format check still assume APA 7th (#1093). Its algorithm now takes the Annotated Bibliography, when present, as the ground truth for citation details, before the orphan check. Prompt-level; unmeasured.

neovim/neovim ↗ 1 release · 1 prerelease
nightlyNvim development (prerelease) build Prerelease2026-11-19T08:47:27Z 
NVIM v0.13.0-dev-2146+g158f7d603e
Build type: RelWithDebInfo
LuaJIT 2.1.1794386141

Release notes

  • Changelog (fixes + features)
  • News (:help news in Nvim)

Install

Windows

Zip

  1. Download nvim-win64.zip (or nvim-win-arm64.zip for ARM)
  2. Extract the zip
  3. Run nvim.exe in your terminal

MSI

  1. Download nvim-win64.msi (or nvim-win-arm64.msi for ARM)
  2. Run the MSI
  3. Run nvim.exe in your terminal

Note: On Windows "Server" you may need to install vcruntime*.dll.

macOS (x86_64)

  1. Download nvim-macos-x86_64.tar.gz
  2. Run xattr -c ./nvim-macos-x86_64.tar.gz (to avoid "unknown developer" warning)
  3. Extract: tar xzvf nvim-macos-x86_64.tar.gz
  4. Run ./nvim-macos-x86_64/bin/nvim

macOS (arm64)

  1. Download nvim-macos-arm64.tar.gz
  2. Run xattr -c ./nvim-macos-arm64.tar.gz (to avoid "unknown developer" warning)
  3. Extract: tar xzvf nvim-macos-arm64.tar.gz
  4. Run ./nvim-macos-arm64/bin/nvim

Linux (x86_64)

If your system does not have the required glibc version, try the (unsupported) builds for older glibc.

AppImage

  1. Download nvim-linux-x86_64.appimage
  2. Run chmod u+x nvim-linux-x86_64.appimage && ./nvim-linux-x86_64.appimage
    • If your system does not have FUSE you can extract the appimage:
      ./nvim-linux-x86_64.appimage --appimage-extract
./squashfs-root/usr/bin/nvim

Tarball

  1. Download nvim-linux-x86_64.tar.gz
  2. Extract: tar xzvf nvim-linux-x86_64.tar.gz
  3. Run ./nvim-linux-x86_64/bin/nvim

Linux (arm64)

AppImage

  1. Download nvim-linux-arm64.appimage
  2. Run chmod u+x nvim-linux-arm64.appimage && ./nvim-linux-arm64.appimage
    • If your system does not have FUSE you can extract the appimage:
      ./nvim-linux-arm64.appimage --appimage-extract
./squashfs-root/usr/bin/nvim

Tarball

  1. Download nvim-linux-arm64.tar.gz
  2. Extract: tar xzvf nvim-linux-arm64.tar.gz
  3. Run ./nvim-linux-arm64/bin/nvim

Other

  • Install by package manager

heygen-com/hyperframes ↗ 6 releases · 4 stable · 2 prerelease
v0.9.70 Stable2026-11-19T07:37:15Z

HyperFrames v0.9.70

Released on 2026-11-19.

Projects with many SVG references or large sets of variables compile faster, and files whose path holds a tab join again. A provider key in your own project's .env file is used again, as the docs describe.

Fixes

  • Media Use: Recipe, prefs and SFX lines show control characters as escapes (36b60d18b4, #8202).
  • Render: Keep segment checkpoints in the local cache (e5283f63b9, #8137).
  • Skills: Raise the launcher minimum to 0.9.68 (a4995e9e6f, #8232).
  • Studio: A sealed preview's timeline shows its text clips' words and look (1cf98cf892, #8228).
  • Parsers: Join files whose path holds a tab again (4abbdc3e0d, #8227).
  • Compiler: Compiling a composition's variables stays fast as the input grows (12b83e9283, #8226).
  • Engine: Files whose path holds a line break are not joined (089e5f6b49, #8219).
  • Producer: Raise the sub-composition expansion limit to 64 MB (4d17b71b4d, #8096).
  • Studio: A sealed preview shows its loading overlay while media still loads (81f89b8150, #8184).
  • CLI: A provider key in your own or a cloned project's .env is used again. Only a project someone sent you gives way to your own key (8ace95ce94, #8099).

Performance

  • Core: Rewriting SVG attribute selectors stays fast with many references (c672ef0e5d, #8216).

Internal

  • Producer: Size the expansion-limit tests from the limit constant (4be5ba2aad, #8223).

HyperFrames v0.9.69

Released on 2026-11-18.

Image captioning in capture can now use a short-lived Vertex access token instead of a service-account key file. In Studio, a script edit in a sealed preview re-runs in place, without reloading the page.

Features

  • Capture: Accept a short-lived Vertex access token for image captioning (e0470b0add, #8215).

Fixes

  • Studio: A script edit re-runs in place in a sealed preview, without a reload (b8bb8b3803, #8158).

v0.9.68 Stable2026-11-18T21:26:40Z

HyperFrames v0.9.68

Released on 2026-11-18.

A render whose page Chrome closed while opening now reopens it, or says why it can't. Sealed previews keep an unsized video's own size and show animated values, and renders can send all their traffic through one outbound proxy.

Features

  • Engine: Renders send page traffic through the egress proxy in HYPERFRAMES_CAPTURE_PROXY (a2830467e, #8196).

Fixes

  • Core: A video with no size of its own keeps its file's size in the preview (e9eac3f9d, #8211).
  • Engine: Reopen a render page whose tab Chrome closed while opening, or say why it can't (d205e5948, #8188).
  • Studio: A sealed resize saves its anchor on the route GSAP moves it by (f149d76ed, #8193).
  • Skills: The launcher's floor is 0.9.67 (855ad35c2, #8205).
  • Studio Server: Proxy joins list pieces by name; a future-dated lock can't hold waiters (fadad3f12, #8204).
  • Studio: A refused timeline delete keeps the server's status in its message (11cc1a519, #8171).
  • Studio: The property panel shows a sealed preview's animated values (f6c9d09de, #8203).
  • CLI: Escape remaining project and sign-in text, ignore stateless callbacks (e83ddaed2, #8177).
  • Studio: Copy, duplicate and audio lane updates work in a sealed preview (30d4cd26c, #8192).
  • Studio: A sealed preview pauses on press and reopens a link's selection (e5566b3dc, #8135).
  • CLI: Reject bad billing --timeout and --needed values as usage errors (559499453, #7910).
  • Producer: Resume and proxy joins reuse only pieces this install made (43582dcb7, #8089).
  • CLI: A render that failed on a video file says to check the file, not to try Docker (72cb2bd9b, #8083).
  • Check: A number with a separator counts as one word in line reading time (7a63b10d7, #8084).
  • Studio: A value or keyframe edit shows in place in a sealed preview (a1ee40094, #8151).

Internal

  • Core: Font lookup tests read only their own fixture fonts, not the machine's (284b28bfc, #8200).

v0.9.67 Stable2026-11-18T15:29:14Z

HyperFrames v0.9.67

Released on 2026-11-18.

A failed render names the missing file again, looking only inside its own folders. Studio's sealed preview fills scenes and clips correctly and previews a 3D slider drag live.

Features

  • Producer: A failed render says which file was gone again, looking only in its own folders (a82ee10c6, #8168).

Fixes

  • Studio: Sealed previews fill scene children, clip parents and first-frame counts (579feb02e, #8180).
  • CLI: The very-many-files received-render test stops timing out on Windows (3db2da06c, #8179).
  • Studio Server: An inserted scene names its file from the project root, as the renderer reads it (f38e1e6de, #8150).
  • Studio: Refused group, freeze frame and animation saves keep the server's status (2efb55ecb, #8178).
  • Producer: Fetch each remote script and stylesheet once, a few at a time, within the size limit (4bdf9181b, #8153).
  • Studio: A 3D slider drag previews live in a sealed preview (75992d82a, #8148).

Performance

  • Core: Count an svg id scope's renames once, not on every stylesheet it rewrites (67410727e, #8182).

code-yeongyu/oh-my-openagent ↗ 2 releases · 2 stable
v5.1.79 Stable2026-11-19T06:08:57Z

Fixed

  • The /remote close in the TUI follows thread_close's close contract (#14234). A close refused because the thread has messages it has not read names /remote close <session> --force and lists those messages, or says the ids are unavailable on that device's engine. The picker then offers "Close anyway". A forced close lists the messages it dropped. The refusal's details.reason is the engine's own reason (turn_active or work_pending). (#14246, fixes #14245) (#14248)
  • Thread tools: a command for a thread whose session parked on another device after it was listed no longer fails unsupported with advice to update omo on that device. The device service answers such a command route_unlisted (its host no longer lists that session, so nothing was sent to it). thread_close now answers closed: false, reason: "not_live", and the other thread tools answer not_resumable, as for a session that ended there. A create, or a reopen's session-file lookup, refused that way is host_unavailable, with a retry. (#14237, fixes #14233) (#14248)
  • The cold-wake session host unit (and every other unit or plist the cold-wake install writes) is now written 0644 under any umask. Under umask 077 it was written 0600, because a file's creation mode is masked by the umask; the mode is now set explicitly before the file is moved into place. (#14249, fixes #14247) (#14248)
  • A cold-wake unit or plist an earlier build already left 0600 is set back to 0644 the next time the install (or a refresh to a newer runtime) finds its content unchanged, with no reload or restart. A file this user cannot change the mode of (another owner's, or on a read-only filesystem) is left as it is and the install still succeeds. (#14249) (#14248)

Changed

  • thread_close no longer drops messages a thread has not read unless you ask it to. Before, it always interrupted the session and silently dropped any messages queued behind its run. Now:
    • A busy thread with unread messages is refused turn_active. The refusal names those messages (pending_deliveries) and gives the force option.
    • thread_close({ thread, force: true }) closes it anyway.
    • Every close result carries dropped_deliveries, the messages it handed back unread (an empty list when there were none).
    • An idle thread still closes without force, even when an interrupted run left messages stranded (#14169); those are listed too.
    • On a device whose engine cannot name its queued messages (older than code-yeongyu/senpi#7006), a default close never interrupts. It says the message ids are unavailable there, and force: true still closes it.
    • omo remote close takes --force and reports the dropped messages as well. (#14242, fixes #14234) (#14248)

Added

  • The desktop can ask OmO which model each category and agent runs. A new read-only extension request, omo.modelRouting.resolve, answers the model every category (under its canonical name) and every task agent would spawn on, using the same resolvers a task call uses against the session's project config, model registry and sign-ins, and says whether that model came from your omo.json or from OmO's defaults. (#14025)
