v5.1.78 Stable2026-11-18T23:57:07Z

Changed

  • Hephaestus on GPT-6.2 models sends one prompt instead of two: the GPT-6.2 core stands alone there, so the Hephaestus role is no longer appended after it. Other models keep the role, and switching models in a session drops or restores it. On a real request the system prompt goes from 9,211 to 4,300 tokens (o200k_base). The refusal-is-final rule is now stated once inside the model-core frame for every agent that carries it instead of at the end of the role, so it survives without the role and is never doubled by a model core that states it. (#14219, part of #14186) (#14225)
  • No behaviour change: tests now pin which ended sessions a fuzzy handoff compares. A fuzzy handoff still compares only the ended sessions that could change its answer. Tests now prove the cut-off, and that a session left out never appears in the list an ambiguous handoff asks you to pick from. (#13190, follows #13141) (#14196)
  • Thread tools in one process share a single endpoint enumeration per agent dir, instead of one per session, so a session that a delivery just started reuses a listing taken within the last 5 s. (#13236, part of #12989) (#14196)
  • Every recovery hint in the thread tools now gives the eval-cell form beside the tool name (thread_list (tool.thread_list() in an eval cell)), the convention the thread tool descriptions already follow. A model that follows a hint no longer hits the "run it in an eval cell" redirect first. (#13240) (#14196)
  • A thread tool call on an account with other devices reads the account's session list once instead of up to three times: overlapping reads share one listing round, and a round that ended under 2 s ago answers the next read (a create, open or session command lists again). The account list waits on every device, 0.5-2 s on a loaded fleet. (#13453) (#14196)
  • thread_list rows carry a state (running, idle, inactive or archived) and the thread's model when known, instead of a per-thread live/alive flag; whether a device is reachable is on devices only. Archived threads are hidden unless a state filter asks for them (omo thread list --state), and a thread closed on this device stays listed as inactive instead of disappearing. status remains for now, derived from the state. (#13469, fixes #13403) (#14196)
  • omo remote takes a device by the name its table prints. The session verbs (open, send, model, interrupt, close) accept a device's id, its name (compared as thread names are) or a unique id prefix, through the same resolver thread_create uses, and their output names the device by its id. A name two online devices share is refused with ambiguous_target, listing each candidate; a name only an offline device has is device_offline; a device nothing matches is not_found. (#13499, #13432) (#14196)
  • The thread control port's setThinkingLevel no longer takes a scope argument that nothing read: both the local and the relay control always change only the addressed session's level (engine scope turn) since #13494. No behavior change. (#13500) (#13506) (#14196)
  • Creating a thread on another of your devices (thread_create with device, omo remote open) is faster. The create used to make four round trips over the relay: it looked up the device's engine endpoint, opened the session, attached to it, and named it. Now it makes two. The endpoint is looked up once per device, nothing attaches to the new session, and the name goes straight to it. (#13889, part of #13404) (#14196)
  • An omo session now reaches this device's device service over one connection instead of two: the thread tools' session commands share the connection their account reads already use, and closing the remote client ends it. (#13944) (#14196)

Fixed

  • omo remote send that waits on a turn now settles inside a bound: --timeout <seconds> (1..600, default 120) answers a typed {kind:"timeout", waited_ms, ...} JSON value instead of hanging when the turn never settles; a session that ends before settling answers {kind:"session_ended", end} as one JSON value under --json (both were a silent hang or stderr-only), and the queued notice rides that same JSON value instead of being dropped (#13425). (#14213) (#14225)
  • thread_create reopens a closed thread on another device again. It failed with EISDIR: the account listing names a session's working folder, never its session file, and that folder was sent to the other device as the file to open (a row with no path sent its working folder too). The reopen now opens the file the close was told by that device, or else asks the device which file carries the thread's id; a device that cannot say answers a typed refusal naming it (unsupported for an engine without the lookup, not_found, or ambiguous_target). A thread this device closed no longer lists as live while the listing catches up, and a reopen answers ok only once the reopened session can be reached, so the next tool call no longer answers not_resumable. (#14179, fixes #14170) (#14225)
  • thread_send to a thread on another device that is not running answers at once instead of waiting about 60 seconds for unsupported (daemon_unavailable). The device holding it is asked first, whether or not this device can wake it there: a thread it holds no session file for is not_found (as the refused reopen says), one it runs is delivered to (also when it was reopened elsewhere since this device last listed it, under whatever key it runs under now), and one it only has on disk is woken when this device can wake it there, else not_resumable, naming the reopen as the next step. A device without the wake capability counts as unable to wake. (#14179, fixes #14184) (#14225)
  • thread_send, thread_read and thread_create aimed at another device no longer wait past what an eval tool call allows: the command waits, wakes, creates and attaches of one call now share one 50 s budget, and a thread_read wait on another device leaves the read after it enough time to return the transcript. A message the relay accepted for the other device but whose answer was lost is reported as the new delivered_unconfirmed (it may have run: read the thread before sending again). (#14185) (#14225)
  • thread_create (and omo remote open) on another device now opens the thread only on that device's own engine (the primary engine endpoint its device service names), never on the first endpoint listed. Before this, a thread could land on an owner shard or on another registered agent dir's older engine, which then refused every cross-device thread_send as unsupported (method_unclassified). When the device names no primary, because its own engine is not listed, the create now fails host_unavailable with reason no_primary_endpoint before anything opens, even if only one other endpoint is listed. The next action says to start or restart omo on that device, and to update the device service on both devices if that does not help (a device service older than 0.1.11 cannot name a primary; behind such a local service a device's lone endpoint is still used). (#14200, fixes #14195) (#14225)
  • thread_send no longer answers not_found for a thread that thread_list shows live while the other device's service is restarting for an update, or when that device missed one account listing round. It now answers a retryable host_unavailable, so the sender can try again instead of treating the thread as gone. (#14174, fixes #14172; this entry was missing from the #14196 train's notes) (#14203) (#14225)
  • A newer gateway store schema of an extension is no longer reported as the gateway's own schema with that extension's migration counts. (#14221) (#14225)
  • When a thread created on another device cannot be named because this device is signed out, the warning now names this device's sign-in instead of telling you to update omo on that device. (#14221) (#14225)
  • thread_create with an alias held by a thread on another device no longer opens a copy of that thread's session file on a third device as if it were the holder. (#14221) (#14225)
  • The remote wake's engine minimum now also holds back a compiled omo or dev build when a caller sets a stricter minimum, and an omo prerelease no longer counts as its release. (#14221) (#14225)
  • A reply's route and the listing it takes are decided from one read of this device's id, and a late endpoint listing no longer replaces a terminal's kind that another session's newer listing recorded. (#14221) (#14225)
  • On Windows, a memory sync and a memory write starting together on a new device no longer fail with a held memory-write lock. Creating the memory repository there takes longer than the 5 s lock wait, so the second caller timed out. Callers in one process now wait for the one creation already running. (#14223, fixes #14188)
  • Memory sync's root-change check no longer reports "unchanged" when the newest retired trust pin is corrupt, truncated or belongs to another account; it reports the check as skipped (PIN_UNREADABLE) instead of comparing against an older pin. A missing pin still falls back to the next older one. (#14218)
  • Input sent over a session's RPC socket (prompt, steer or follow_up) now counts as typed input for the thread tools. A run it starts or joins never claims the input as a delivery it received: when the client reused the id of a real delivery that this session never received, thread_send was refused invalid_arguments (guard: "unknown_cause") or continued a chain the session was not part of. Such a send now starts a new causal chain, and a run the gateway started from a bound thread keeps that thread's chain (#12317). (#12318) (#14196)
  • thread_create that opens an existing thread and passes the alias that thread already holds ({thread: X, alias: X}, or the thread's id with its own alias) now opens it and keeps the alias, instead of failing alias_taken with the thread itself named as the holder. An alias held by another thread is still alias_taken. (#12732) (#14175) (#14196)
  • When another device refuses a thread_send or thread_handoff because the chain went past that device user's thread chain limit (threads.chainCountLimit), the tool now answers chain_count_limit, naming the device and the setting, instead of unsupported with advice to update omo on that device. A queued delivery to a session there that is not running maps a chain-limit refusal from that device's gateway store to the same code, with the chain count and the limit, and the receiving consumer now passes on the store's own refusal reason (for example sender_not_allowed) instead of the coarse code. (#12766) (#14196)
  • A send to another device whose state read gets no answer can be retried right away. When the get_state read that a steer to another device starts with ends without an answer, thread_send now reports host_unavailable (nothing was sent there, retry is safe) instead of idempotency_uncertain, and a retry under the same idempotency_key reads the target again instead of replaying that failure; a send that fails before its record leaves this device keeps no receipt. (#12947, fixes #12925, part of #12879) (#14196)
  • A delivery message without a valid id no longer inherits the previous sender's reply allowance. A session_control_delivery whose delivery_id is not a string is malformed: after a final answer it starts a new group with nobody approved, and while work is running it joins the work without adding a sender or changing the hop - it no longer passes as a turn the engine started (which kept the earlier group's allowance). The same-account bound of the reply allowance is stated where the rule runs (component.ts noteInput and the thread AGENTS.md), not only in the release notes. (#13134) (#14196)
  • A thread named by its alias after a late sign-in is still answered when the rename fails, and says why. When the account is gone by the alias claim and the rename that names the new thread by the alias fails with an untyped error, thread_create now answers ok with the created thread and local_name_unavailable: "host_unavailable", instead of an error that did not name the thread. The error's message comes back as local_name_unavailable_reason, cut to 300 characters with an ellipsis, so a failure can be traced without a whole host reply filling the result. (#13185, follows #13179; folds #13283 and #13284) (#14196)
  • A thread tool call no longer waits forever behind an endpoint enumeration (host status --all) that never answers: after 30 s the next call starts a fresh one and the stuck answer is dropped. (#13231, fixes #13009) (#14196)
  • Text from another device prints escaped in the thread tools' output. A thread title, project path or refusal reason that another device sends is shown with its control, format and line-separator characters escaped (\u202e, \^[, ...) wherever thread_list, thread_read, thread_create and the control tools print it, including the candidates of an ambiguous name; nothing is deleted, and addressing a thread by its exact title still finds it. (#13305, #13304) (#14196)
  • Thread tools: a command for a session that ended on another device since it was listed now fails with not_resumable ("the thread is not running on device X") instead of unsupported with advice to update omo on that device. (#13439) (#14196)
  • Thread tools (thread_list, thread_read, thread_send, thread_create, ...) and omo thread no longer run host status --all on every call. They read the endpoint registry on disk instead, so a local thread_list that took about 9 s on a host with 50 endpoints answers in well under a second. (#13452) (#14196)
  • Listing a thread endpoint that stopped answering reads its leftover session claims asynchronously, so an endpoint with many stale claims no longer blocks the engine's event loop while thread_list runs (fixes #13551, folded from #13573). (#13452) (#14196)
  • A reply to a sender named by its device no longer goes through the relay when this device's own id cannot be read; the send is refused host_unavailable with the reason, and nothing is sent. (#13483, part of #13037) (#14196)
  • A session that another device holds but does not run is reached through a probe only while this device's service can carry the wake; with the wake off, the answer stays not_found instead of an attach refusal. (#13483) (#14196)
  • A wake whose connection drops after the request was written is reported uncertain, not refused, so it is never retried as if nothing ran. The list of local API codes that refuse before sending now lives once in device-service-core and is shared with the thread tools. (#13483) (#14196)
  • When another device's service refuses a wake with free-form text, that text is no longer shown; the reason reads unrecognised, as it already did for the receiving consumer's refusals. (#13483, part of #13277) (#14196)
  • thread_list drops the cached rows of a device the account no longer lists, so thread_create and thread_send answer not_found for that device's thread instead of addressing it; an offline device keeps its rows, and with no account device list the filter is a no-op. (#13539) (#14196)
  • Listing a thread endpoint that stopped answering reads its leftover claims and the session files they name asynchronously and one at a time, so a host that died holding many sessions neither blocks the engine's event loop nor runs it out of file descriptors while thread_list runs. Endpoints are still listed together, so the files open at once are bounded by the number of endpoints, not by the number of stale claims. (#13581, fixes #13579 and #13584) (#14196)
  • Cold wake: a message from another device now wakes sessions on installs of the compiled omo binary (the one-line installer) too. The wake checked the engine version in a form only npm installs report, so it stayed off on compiled installs (#13672). (#13673) (#14196)
  • Cold wake: a message that arrives while the woken session is still being restored now runs once the restore settles. It no longer waits for you to open the session (#13708). (#13709) (#14196)
  • thread_read with wait_ms on a thread on another device no longer settles early while the session retries after a provider error: the retry's auto_retry_start / auto_retry_end records now count as the run still going, so the wait ends on the retried run's turn (#13871). (#14196)
  • An agent whose omo build is older than the one that last upgraded this machine's gateway store no longer gets an opaque internal_error from thread_send and the other thread tools (or omo thread send). The store is still refused, so nothing is read or written at a schema the build does not know, and the failure is now gateway_schema_too_new: it names the store's schema and the build's schema, and tells you to update omo with the exact command (omo update), then restart the session. (#13941, fixes #13927) (#14196)
  • Thread tools: a call toward another device that this device cannot send because its own relay sign-in lapsed (sign_in_required, signed_out) now fails with host_unavailable asking to sign in on this device, instead of unsupported with advice to update omo on the other device. (#14015) (#14196)
  • thread_read with a small max_bytes now returns the newest messages that fit, ending at the latest reply, on this device and on another one. Before, it filled the budget from the oldest message of the 60-message window, so a 1.5 KB read of a short thread returned only its preamble. next_cursor from such a read pages the older messages of the window. (#14176) (#14196)
  • When the host refuses to close a thread, the thread_close refusal (unsupported) now names the engine's reason in its message, for example refused to close it (work_pending: an admitted delivery not yet written). Before, the message named only the reason code, so a caller could not tell a running turn, a bash command and an undelivered message apart. The engine side of the same bug, where an interrupted run left a delivery that kept every close refused, is fixed in code-yeongyu/senpi#6971. (#14178, fixes #14169) (#14196)
  • Remote sessions: a session host unit written by an older omo (KeepAlive / Restart=always, naming an older ~/.omo/binary-runtime/<version>/omo) is brought to the launched runtime's run-once shape and program on the first launch of a newer runtime, keeping its own agent directory and PATH, never re-enabling a disabled unit and never blocking the launch; a refused ensure no longer respawns every 30 s. omo daemon run now says why the engine refused, on which socket and which host holds it. (#14194, fixes #14191)
  • Codex edition recon agents: explorer and librarian bundled TOMLs default to gpt-6-luna at low on the fast service tier instead of the flagship gpt-6-astra, matching the cheap recon routing every other edition's model tables already pin (#8616). Untouched installs step down on the next sync through the model-receipt mechanism; a hand-set model or effort keeps surviving the move. The other ten roles keep Astra at their current efforts. (#14192, fixes #14189)
  • Memory sync: when the newest retired trust pin cannot be read, the root-change check is reported as skipped instead of silently comparing against an older pin; the recovery-phrase terminal reader zeroes its buffer when released. (#14105, fixes #14098)
  • Memory sync: a join, enable or passkey recovery that already pinned the account is no longer reported as failed when the follow-up root-change check cannot read the local retired pins. The check is reported as skipped instead of passing silently: omo sync enable prints it with the error code, the desktop's enable result carries an optional rootCheck: { kind: "skipped", reason } beside rootChanged, the in-app warning goes to the memory component's log rather than the terminal, and recovery emits sync.trust.root_check_skipped. (#13641, fixes #12382) (#14097)
  • Memory sync: creating a recovery phrase now checks the sync service's own answer on whether it accepts recovery-phrase keys, read fresh through the signed-in session for every creation. Before this the host never wired that check, so a recovery phrase could not be created even where the service accepts one. A refused, absent, unreadable or signed-out answer still refuses before any words are shown or anything is written. (#13646, fixes #13631) (#14097)
  • Memory sync works again after a recovery phrase is set up. The sync engine's requests (batches, objects, uploads, sync state and the event stream), the device-list read and the account reads now declare the client contract version and the recovery-phrase capability, as the account and pairing requests already did. Before this, the service refused every one of them with "client update required" for an account with a recovery phrase, so no device synced (#13691). (#13693) (#14097)
  • Memory sync no longer stops for good when its first start fails. A start that fails (for example another omo process holding the account's credential lock past the 15 s wait) or gets stuck is retried on the same backoff as a waiting start, 15 s doubling to five minutes. A stuck start is named in the log, and the first successful start logs "memory sync started", so a log shows whether sync is running (#13701). (#13706) (#14097)
  • Memory sync: a device that has turned sync on and joined the account now receives the account's memory without writing anything first. Its memory repository is created through the same first-write path a memory write uses, only for a joined device with sync on; a device that has not joined, a device with sync off and every read still create nothing. (#14096, fixes #14094) (#14097)

Added

  • thread_archive { thread, archived? } archives or unarchives a thread on any device through the engine's session flag. (#13469, part of #13407) (#14196)

  • Scripts can create, interrupt, close, rename and re-model threads through the thread SDK. createThreadSdk(...) from runtime/thread-sdk/sdk.js now has create, interrupt, close, rename and set_model. They take the same arguments as thread_create, thread_interrupt, thread_close, thread_rename and thread_set_model, run the same code, and answer the same data on this device or another device of the account. A script acts as itself, not as a session, so self answers caller_context_missing and a thread it creates records no creator session. (#13413) (#13495) (#14196)

  • thread_list rows of a held thread carry the engine's activity (idle, running, awaiting_approval, queued or errored), error_code with errored, and turn_id when the engine reports them (senpi#6688). state folds them: queued and awaiting_approval are running, errored is idle; inactive and archived rows carry none. (#13504) (#14196)

  • Memory sync can be turned off on one device. omo sync disable turns it off for the device it runs on: that device stops sending and receiving memory, memory already on it stays, and the account's other devices keep syncing. omo sync enable turns it on again without a new approval. An OmO session already running on that device stops syncing when its sync next reports a change, or at its next start, and a process that turns sync on again starts the engines it had stopped right away. For the desktop, the engine answers omo.memory.sync.disable with { kind: "off" } or a typed problem, and lists "disable" in omo.memory.sync.features; turning off from the desktop app needs an app release that carries the matching request. (#14121)

  • The memory sync status can say how many memories changed while this device was offline: status.sync.appliedWhileOffline is the number of memories the catch-up updated after the device was away (OmO was closed, or sync could not reach the service), 0 when nothing changed, and absent when the latest changes arrived while it was online. (#14121)

  • omo sync enable and omo sync recover can use a recovery phrase on the terminal itself: words appear only on the controlling terminal, never in output or logs, and the flow refuses under an agent harness, CI or without a terminal. Creating a phrase still needs the client flag, and the CLI asks the service whether it accepts phrase keys before each creation. (#13337) (#14097)

Cua Driver 0.54.1-nightly.20261119.44457756598 (nightly)

Automated immutable nightly built from an exact main commit. Nightlies are opt-in, may be less stable than regular releases, and never replace stable update discovery.

Source

  • Commit: 052dc3a0c19646f89d5e6ca30e110e2dd541eec2
  • Changes: nightly-cua-driver-rs-v0.53.1-nightly.20261118.44236707997…052dc3a0c196

Changes

  • Fix: report macOS AX enumeration counts in ax_window_unresolved. (#8096) Thanks @lorenzozanee; reported by @JayLewisLLC.
  • Fix: publish display-only rows as observations so verify_state can read them. (#7566)
  • Fix: run a background pixel-targeted menu key equivalent on macOS. (#8081) reported by @howardxie-dev.
  • Fix: explain session-scoped snapshots in the stale token error. (#8110) Thanks @Alx8g.
  • Fix: add started_at_unix_ms to recording session.json. (#8118) Thanks @Iflaqbhat; reported by @methanoya.
  • Feat: report is_password for Windows Edit elements. (#8124) Thanks @WingedGuardian.
  • Fix: exit the macOS daemon when an update replaces its app. (#8135) reported by @gabrielhamalwa.
  • Test: pin Windows posted clicks against an SDL-style mouse leave. (#8134) reported by @DAOZHENREN.
  • Fix: fall back to WARP for unusable Windows captures. (#8131) Thanks @SDSLeon.
  • Fix: wait 40 ms after a foreground Windows desktop click and accept a hidden owned dialog. (#8160) Thanks @CMGS.

Contributors

Thanks to @Alx8g, @CMGS, @DAOZHENREN, @gabrielhamalwa, @howardxie-dev, @Iflaqbhat, @JayLewisLLC, @lorenzozanee, @methanoya, @SDSLeon, @WingedGuardian for contributing to or reporting changes in this nightly.

cua-driver-rs-v0.54.0cua-driver-rs: v0.54.0 Prerelease2026-11-18T18:22:34Z

Cua Driver 0.54.0

Summary

This release adds 1 features and includes 3 fixes.

Features

  • report is_password for Windows Edit elements. (#8124) Thanks @WingedGuardian.

Fixes

  • exit the macOS daemon when an update replaces its app. (#8135) reported by @gabrielhamalwa.
  • fall back to WARP for unusable Windows captures. (#8131) Thanks @SDSLeon.
  • wait 40 ms after a foreground Windows desktop click and accept a hidden owned dialog. (#8160) Thanks @CMGS.

Contributors

Thanks to @CMGS, @gabrielhamalwa, @SDSLeon, @WingedGuardian for contributing to this release.

Install

macOS and Linux:

/bin/bash -c "$(curl -fsSL https://cua.ai/driver/install.sh)"

Windows:

irm https://cua.ai/driver/install.ps1 | iex

Cua Driver ships universal macOS binaries, x86_64 and arm64 Windows builds, and Linux preview builds. Release assets also include tag-pinned installer and uninstaller scripts.

Why GitHub says “Pre-release”

GitHub's label is used only to keep this monorepo's repository-wide “Latest” pointer from switching between independently released products. A plain Cua Driver SemVer is a stable release; npm and PyPI publish it on their normal stable channels, and the installers resolve the versioned cua-driver-rs-v* releases directly.

open-gsd/gsd-core ↗ 1 release · 1 stable
v1.22.0 Stable2026-11-19T04:04:25Z

Install

npm i @opengsd/gsd-core@1.22.0
# or
npm i @opengsd/gsd-core@latest

What's Changed

Added

  • STATE.md size warnings — quick-tasks-append now reports a warnings entry (exit 0, row still written in full) when a task description is over 240 characters (quick-batch complete returns the same warnings for the row it appends), and /gsd-health adds the advisory warning W031 when STATE.md is more than 3x its 100-line target, pointing to state prune and milestone archive-quick. /gsd-quick and /gsd-fast pass a one-line title, and the STATE.md line target now reads 100 everywhere (#5830). (#5882)
  • A plan can declare executor_runtime, and a new opt-in gate and model keys are registered — phase-plan-index now emits executor_runtime for each plan (null when unset), verify plan-structure refuses it on a plan with autonomous: false, a checkpoint:* task or an agent_hint, and workflow.cross_runtime_execution (default false) plus execute.models.<runtime> are accepted by config-set. Nothing dispatches on them yet; the dispatch lands with the cross-runtime executor work (#3711). (#6302) (#6307)

Changed

  • resolve_model_ids: "omit" no longer strips profile routing on Claude Code — with an explicit project "omit", every agent spawned with a blank model, so Claude Code ran it on CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL or the session model and a mapper profiled to a cheap tier ran on the orchestrator tier. On runtimes that accept tier aliases, resolve-model and the init payloads now return the profile tier alias (opus / sonnet / haiku) under "omit", and dynamic_routing escalates by those aliases instead of tier_models IDs; every other runtime keeps the blank. (#3703) (#6217)
  • gsd-tools starts faster for commands that need little of it — it loaded about 220 of its library modules on every call, whatever the command; it now loads each module the first time a command uses it, so a call like runtime-identity loads a few dozen. What each command does, prints and writes, and its exit code, are unchanged; a stack trace from an unexpected error shows one more frame. (#5865) (#6098)
  • /gsd-new-project and /gsd-settings now ask which executors may run your plans — choose gsd-executor only, or allow other executor agents installed on your runtime that a plan names with agent_hint: (the default); new project configs now record the choice as workflow.agent_hint_routing instead of leaving it implicit (#6211). (#6225)

Fixed

  • The secret-read guard test no longer contradicts the grep-pattern rule — two rows in the regex-escape suite still expected a grep pattern operand to be blocked after #5884 made it a name, not a file read, which turned the base Tests run red. (#5884) (#6255)
  • Onboarding's summary commit now triggers the opt-in graph rebuild — it committed through gsd_run --cwd "$ONBOARDING_ROOT" query commit, a global-flag form the graphify hook intentionally does not recognize, so no rebuild ran after onboarding. The workflow now changes into the onboarding root and runs the unflagged gsd_run query commit, so the existing gated rebuild (opt-in, default branch, CI, binary, live lock) runs against the onboarded checkout. (#5935) (#6267)
  • Windsurf uninstall and reinstall keep a symlinked hooks.json — uninstall deleted the link and a reinstall with changes replaced it with a plain file, leaving GSD's hook entries in the file it pointed at, whatever GSD_ALLOW_SYMLINKED_DEST said; now a symlinked hooks.json is refused (nothing removed or changed) unless the opt-in is set, and with it the file the link points at is edited and the link stays. (#5978) (#6346)
  • The branch-protection guide now documents the Actions event policy that pull_request_target needs — GitHub enforces its default block on that event on December 2, 2026 and blocked it in scheduled windows before then, which left the three required PR checks unreported and kept PRs out of the merge queue. The new section gives the symptom, why the workflows are not changed, the policy payload and how to recover a PR. (#6253) (#6254)
  • Copilot uninstall and install no longer unlink or write through a symlinked AGENTS.md or copilot-instructions.md unasked — a dotfiles-managed link holding only the GSD block was deleted (leaving the target untouched), and a mixed one was edited through the link, with no regard for GSD_ALLOW_SYMLINKED_DEST. Without the opt-in both uninstall and the install merge now refuse before any change and say how to opt in; with it the link is kept, a mixed file loses only the GSD block and a GSD-only target is emptied rather than deleted. Regular files behave as before. (#5946) (#6268)
  • Capability hooks registered at execute:pre now run — /gsd-execute-phase declared the execute:pre Loop Extension Point but never dispatched it, so a step, contribution or blocking gate installed there was silently skipped; the host now renders and dispatches it once before the first wave (a blocking gate stops execution before any plan runs), and the loop-host contract build now fails when a workflow declares a point it never renders. (#5848) (#6082)
  • Plugin installs and updates ship a built runtime library — after claude plugin install or claude plugin update, gsd-tools no longer starts with an unbuilt gsd-core/bin/lib that the self-heal cannot compile without typescript; the marketplace entry now installs a prebuilt plugin branch that each stable release publishes (#5796). (#6228)
  • Loop-hook skill steps now show invocable identifiers — an agent following a rendered step now receives the skill identifier it can invoke instead of a bare name no skill answers to; registry and JSON ref.skill values remain bare. (#5397)
  • The npm-audit gate no longer blames a PR for advisories that next introduced — on pull_request runs the audit baseline was pinned to pull_request.base.sha, which can be older than the base commit the merge ref contains, so a package next itself made vulnerable read as introduced by the PR. The baseline now advances to the newest base commit the tested tree contains, with the pin as its floor. (#5897) (#6259)
  • Git capability installs now honor pinned refs — bare tags, typed tags, non-default branches, and full typed commit SHAs stage the requested tree even when it is behind the default tip; tip-local commit-ish spellings keep their prior shallow-checkout behavior, unsafe normalized refs and empty/invalid typed pins fail before retrieval, and Git hooks stay inactive during staging. #sha: now requires a full 40- or 64-hex object ID; short IDs and branch names are rejected, including when re-resolving previously recorded sources. Pinned installs of large repositories no longer time out during checkout, installs work when Git's clone.defaultRemoteName is set, and refs containing *, ? or [ are rejected. Update status identifies invalid typed pins instead of presenting them as safe pins. (#5408)
  • state record-session and other STATE.md writes no longer inflate total_phases from heading text quoted in prose — a ### Phase N: inside a code span, a blockquote, a fenced block or a sentence was counted as a phase, so progress.total_phases rose and percent fell. validate consistency raised phantom "in ROADMAP.md but no directory on disk" warnings for the same quoted headings. Only real level 2–4 ATX headings count now, so a ##### Phase N: line, ###Phase N: with no space, and a four-space-indented ### Phase N: (an indented code block) are no longer read as phases. (#5880) (#6291)
  • resolve-execution --attempt 0 now reports the same model as resolve-model — with dynamic_routing enabled on a runtime with its own tier map (Codex, OpenCode), or with a model_policy preset or a model_profile_overrides.claude pin on Claude Code, the first spawn resolved through resolve-execution (and the provider-escalation ladder) used dynamic_routing.tier_models while resolve-model and the init payloads reported the runtime or policy model. A respawn that does not move the tier now gets that same model too; a real escalation still uses tier_models. (#6214) (#6219)
  • An agentless Codex install no longer makes every reinstall report phantom local patches — the install hashed its file manifest before the Codex @~/.claude/gsd-core/ include rewrite (and the Kimi/Copilot/Antigravity include retarget) changed those files, and only the agent-roles branch re-hashed afterwards, so reinstalls backed up ~100 untouched files into gsd-local-patches/ and told the user to run $gsd-update --reapply; the manifest is now re-hashed after the rewrites. (#5954) (#6314)
  • Registry submission docs now state the approved-issue requirement — a contributor following the registry README, the registry-entry PR template or the reviewer-lane and take-over how-tos no longer opens a registry PR that the unsolicited-PR gate auto-closes. (#5410)
  • Graphs now rebuild after commits made by GSD workflows — unflagged query commit calls through the shipped GSD launchers now trigger auto-update under the existing opt-in, default-branch, and live-lock rules. Global-flag forms remain excluded until the hook can resolve their target checkout safely (#5935). (#5399)
  • /gsd-audit-uat no longer reports All Clear for an audit that scanned no files — the all-clear fired whenever there were no items and no parse gaps, and total_files counts only files that held something to report, so a tree the scan never reached read the same as a tree of clean files; audit-fix could likewise report zero findings from a tree it never walked. audit-uat now reports summary.files_scanned (every UAT, VERIFICATION and deferred-items file it read), /gsd-audit-uat and /gsd-audit-fix print Nothing Scanned when it is 0, and a clean audit prints how many files it scanned. (#6231) (#6294)
  • Cline global uninstall no longer deletes or rewrites a symlinked ~/.agents/AGENTS.md unasked — a dotfiles-managed link holding only the GSD block was removed (leaving the target untouched) and a mixed one was edited through the link, with no regard for GSD_ALLOW_SYMLINKED_DEST, all with exit 0 and no notice. Without the opt-in the uninstall now leaves the link and its target alone and says it skipped the file; with it the link is kept, a mixed file loses only the GSD block and a GSD-only target is emptied rather than deleted. Regular files behave as before, and the symlink helpers introduced for Copilot are shared rather than duplicated. (#5947) (#6275)
  • pi, vscode and zcode are now recognised as the install's runtime identity — the runtime name policy did not list these three registered runtimes, so GSD_RUNTIME or the install marker naming one of them never set runtime in the loaded config, and under resolve_model_ids: "omit" pi lost its own tier map. (#3703) (#6217)
  • The CI timeout report no longer records a job that was cancelled while still waiting for a runner as a full-length run — such a job has a started_at (when it was queued) and a later completed_at (when it was cancelled), so its queue wait was reported as run time: the near-cap report put it over 100% of its cap and the Windows shard-balance check added a bogus sample that skewed a shard's p90 and the median gap. The shared duration rule now returns no duration for a cancelled job with an empty steps list, runner id 0 and an empty runner name, so both consumers skip it; a cancelled job that ran, including one killed at its timeout cap, and a record that lacks those fields are still counted. (#6177) (#6295)
  • /gsd-quick-batch --resume no longer refuses a batch as "base revision diverged" after the batch's own merges — the batch recorded the git HEAD it was created against and never advanced it, while its own executor commits, wave merges and final docs commit move HEAD, so resuming any batch that had merged an item was refused. The batch now records the HEAD it leaves behind after each of those steps (quick-batch update --head-revision), and resume compares the live HEAD to that recorded head; a HEAD moved by anything the batch did not record, an unknown batch id and a batch with no base revision behave as before. (#6203) (#6296)
  • Workflow, agent and reference shell fences resolve project and workstream paths — under GSD_PROJECT=<name> or a workstream, the fences now read and commit the scoped STATE, ROADMAP, REQUIREMENTS, phases, quick, milestone and config files instead of the root project's, and writer verbs in those fences forward the workstream token so a scoped tracking commit is no longer empty. (#5861) (#6306)
  • Verification scaffolds no longer fail status reads — omit the unfinished report status so the reader routes to execute-phase while phase completion remains blocked until verification passes. (#5398)
  • The statusline context meter now reads a k/M suffix on CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW — 500k was parsed as 500 and clamped to the 100k minimum, so the meter hit 100% at 67,000 tokens instead of 467,000 and the context monitor's warnings fired early. A case-insensitive k (x1,000) or M (x1,000,000) on an integer or decimal now resolves the way Claude Code reads it, then the existing 100k–1M clamp applies; plain digits, grouped digits and other values behave as before. (#6131) (#6297)
  • state record-session and state add-decision no longer zero progress.total_plans after a milestone close — when .planning/phases/ exists but is empty (the shape milestone complete leaves), the stored plan counters are kept, the same as when the directory is absent. (#6279) (#6290)
  • Local reviewer lanes no longer report an unrelated default-port service as available — with review.llama_cpp_host, review.lm_studio_host or review.ollama_host unset, an unrelated local service answering 404, 401 or a redirect on :8080, :1234 or :11434 made the lane look available and the review prompt was sent to it; a default host now counts as available only when it answers /v1/models with an OpenAI-style models listing, while a host you configure keeps the any-answer rule. (#5963) (#6330)
  • /gsd-new-milestone and /gsd-new-project under a workstream or a named project now write and commit that scope's roadmap files, not the root project's — the roadmapper armed the write guard for the root .planning/ROADMAP.md and STATE.md, so the scoped roadmap Write was blocked (or, on a new project, a 15-minute unlock for the root ROADMAP.md was left on disk), and the workflows never told it where to write. The roadmapper and revise spawns now pass the init-resolved ROADMAP.md, STATE.md and REQUIREMENTS.md paths in a <write_targets> block, the roadmapper arms the guard only for those paths, init new-project reports state_path, and /gsd-new-project commits the scoped ROADMAP.md and STATE.md. The research synthesizer and the #222 self-heal now follow the research directory init reports (research_dir) instead of a literal .planning/research/, and verify-summary accepts that absolute path. (#5859) (#6292)
  • /gsd-code-review --fix --auto now commits the converged REVIEW.md even when REVIEW-FIX.md is invalid or missing — the re-review loop rewrote the tracked REVIEW.md but only the valid-report path committed it, so the tree stayed dirty and /gsd-ship asked to commit or stash. A malformed REVIEW-FIX.md is still never committed, and when git sees the malformed report, the warning now says it keeps /gsd-ship asking to commit or stash until it is fixed and committed, or deleted. (#5527) (#5541)
  • validate health --ws checks and repairs the workstream's own milestone archive — W018 and its --repair backfill compared and wrote the root MILESTONES.md/milestones/ even though milestone complete --ws archives into the workstream, so repairing one workstream edited the shared root file and the workstream's own archive was never checked; both now use the same project/workstream-scoped pair. (#5881) (#6313)
  • The debugger-spawn check reads dollar-quoting — claude --agent $'gsd-debugger', $'gsd\x2ddebugger', $"gsd-debugger" and a launch inside a bash -c $'…' body or an eval $'…' operand are now denied as debugger-cli-spawn, as the unquoted name already was. The check reads a command the way the secret-read scan does: under bash's, zsh's and dash's readings, then the plain one, for any command that, once its \<newline> continuations are joined, holds $', $", $${ or $$(, or a quote together with (( or $[. That test is made again on each substitution body, eval operand and bash -c body the scan reads. Any other command, none of whose bodies holds one either, is scanned exactly as before. (#5441) (#6183)
  • The shared test-project fixture no longer follows the host's init.defaultBranch — its repo now starts on master regardless of the developer machine's git config, so tests that ask about master (K2, #4834) pass locally on a stock macOS git as they do on CI. (#5913) (#6261)
  • Quick-batch's orchestrator-worktree dispatch now gives every executor-prompt placeholder a value for a quick item — it reused execute-phase's isolation prompt "verbatim" but named values for only three of the seven placeholders, so {phase_number}, {phase_name}, {plan_id} and {plan_padded} could reach the executor prompt and the worker-record summary path as literal text. A quick item now states {plan_padded} as its quick id, marks the three phase-only placeholders not applicable, uses Execute quick item <id>. as the header, and omits the dispatch marker line. (#5934) (#6265)
  • A local Claude uninstall now removes GSD's hook registrations, statusline entry and permission entries from .claude/settings.local.json — a local install writes them there (#338), but uninstall only ever opened settings.json and skipped the cleanup when that file was absent, so after the hook scripts were deleted Claude Code kept invoking commands that no longer existed. Uninstall now cleans the per-scope settings file the install chose and still cleans settings.json for installs that predate the routing; user hooks (including ones sharing a group with a GSD hook), user permissions and other keys stay, and an unparseable local settings file is left untouched with a skip notice. (#5952) (#6274)
  • TDD RED evidence no longer blocks the wave merge — tdd.md told the executor to write the RED record inside the project without naming a place, and an untracked evidence file made worktree.cleanup-wave refuse the merge as worktree_dirty; the reference now names the self-ignoring .gsd/tdd-evidence/{phase}-{plan}/ directory and requires confirming it is ignored before the first write. (#5879) (#6311)
  • A decimal phase written 02.1 in its heading and 2.1 in the checklist or progress table is now one phase, not two — the milestone phase-id scan kept both spellings, so smart-entry reported an inflated position and total (Phase 5 of 5 for four phases) and the roadmap phase list repeated the phase. The first-seen spelling is kept; custom, letter-suffixed and milestone-prefixed ids keep their own identities. (#5895) (#6252)
  • /gsd:code-review --fix run from a linked worktree no longer merges the fixes into the main checkout's branch — the fixer fast-forwarded the first git worktree list entry (the main checkout, usually main) instead of the worktree that has the reviewed branch checked out, so the fixes landed on the wrong branch or were stranded on the temp branch with a warning that named the wrong one. (#5876) (#6309)
  • /gsd-new-milestone --reset-phase-numbers no longer archives the root project's phase folders from inside a named project or workstream — the reset fence in reset-phase-safety.md moved the literal .planning/phases and then checked that same folder, so under GSD_PROJECT or --ws it moved the root project's phase directories and left the scoped ones in place. It now moves and checks ${phases_dir}; flat projects are unchanged. (#5860) (#6288)
  • record-dispatch-isolation no longer hangs when .gsd/.gitignore is a FIFO — the ignore file is now opened without blocking, so a non-regular ignore file is skipped and the isolation sentinel is still recorded. (#5857) (#6285)
  • config-get commit_docs now reports the value the runtime uses — after config-set planning.commit_docs false, config-get commit_docs read "Key not found", so the workflow gates that read it (/gsd-quick's pre-dispatch commit, /gsd-new-milestone and eight others) fell back to committing .planning/ files. commit_docs and search_gitignored now read back the resolved value under either spelling (commit_docs or planning.commit_docs), including a gitignored .planning/ resolving commit_docs to false, so the /gsd-settings integrations screen also shows a nested planning.search_gitignored: true correctly. Reading the nested spelling can therefore print a value other than the one written there: with both commit_docs: true and planning.commit_docs: false in config.json, config-get planning.commit_docs prints true, because the flat key wins and that is the value the runtime applies. For these two settings --default no longer applies: the read prints the resolved value and exits 0, so with no .planning/ directory config-get commit_docs --default false now prints the default, true unless ~/.gsd/defaults.json sets it, where it printed false. A config file the read depends on that is not a valid JSON object or cannot be read fails the read, --default or not, wherever the runtime reports that file unusable: for example the active scope's config.json (the project's, the workstream's under GSD_WORKSTREAM, or the GSD_PROJECT one), the root config.json under a workstream, or ~/.gsd/defaults.json when there is no .planning/ directory. The runtime does not report it in one case, and the read then prints the built-in default as the runtime resolves it: with both GSD_PROJECT and GSD_WORKSTREAM set and a usable root config.json, when the runtime falls back from the workstream it reports no fault in what that fallback reads (the GSD_PROJECT config.json, or ~/.gsd/defaults.json when that project has no directory). (#5470) (#5502)
  • Kimi CLI and Kimi Code install and uninstall no longer delete or replace a symlinked config.toml unasked — uninstall removed the link of a GSD-only file (leaving the target with the GSD block) or replaced the link with a regular file, and install over a link to a user-owned file replaced the link too, with exit 0 and no notice, whatever GSD_ALLOW_SYMLINKED_DEST said. Without the opt-in both now refuse and leave the link and its target untouched, naming the opt-in; with it the managed block is written into or stripped from the link's target, the link is kept and a GSD-only target is emptied rather than unlinked. A dangling link is never replaced or created through, and regular files behave as before. (#5949) (#6276)
  • A hard kill while gsd updates .gsd/.gitignore no longer leaves an untracked temp file in git status — the ignore-file merge now stages its replacement inside a self-ignoring directory and the owner adds a /.gitignore.tmp-* rule. (#5856) (#6283)
  • A plan routed to a specialist executor with agent_hint: is now held to the same worktree isolation and model rules as gsd-executor — before, the isolation guards let a hinted executor run in the primary checkout even when the phase required a worktree, it got gsd-executor's model instead of its own model_overrides entry, and a hint naming a GSD role such as gsd-verifier was dispatched as the executor; such hints are now refused with a warning; while a fail-closed case holds (for example a malformed record file), host built-in agents such as Explore are also held to the worktree rule (#6213). (#6220)
  • Git-source capability installs are no longer staged as embedded repositories — omit the temporary clone’s .git metadata while preserving checkout files, validation, and consent hashing. A Git bundle’s content hash is recomputed from the metadata-free checkout when that bundle is next installed or upgraded. (#5407)
  • A project-coded or unpadded phase directory now resolves its own NN-UAT.md and NN-VERIFICATION.md — in a directory such as XR-03-demo or 5-demo the resolver looked only for a file named after the directory's own token, so a sibling that sorts first (03-HUMAN-UAT.md, 03-CORRECTION-VERIFICATION.md) won, and a passed phase could read as missing with route execute-phase. The resolver now also tries the code-stripped, zero-padded name the scaffolder writes, after a file that literally carries the directory's token. (#5931) (#6263)
  • A project-local agents directory or manifest that is a symlink or junction is now followed — init new-project and init new-milestone reported agents_installed: false and fell back to the global agents dir for a project that links one shared GSD install, because the local probe used lstat. A link now resolves to the local install when a manifest is present, and a dangling link still falls back to the global dir. (#5911) (#6260)
  • requirements mark-complete and phase complete now advance a traceability row whose Status carries a note — a cell such as Pending (blocked on an upstream decision) was refused, so mark-complete rolled the checkbox back and reported the requirement not_found although its checkbox and row both existed, and phase complete skipped the row silently while still reporting an update. mark-complete, revert-phase and phase complete now read the status word at the start of the cell (the rule the ROADMAP Status reader already uses), so the annotated row reaches Complete and a Complete (note) row is recognized as complete and can be reverted; Blocked, Deferred, Completed and a word glued onto the status (Pendingx) are still refused. (#6238) (#6293)
  • init.new-project and init.new-milestone now resolve research and milestone history inside a named project or workstream — under GSD_PROJECT, research_dir named the root .planning/research, and under GSD_PROJECT or --ws the latest_completed_milestone and phase_archive_path fields read the root MILESTONES.md, so a scoped run read the root project's research and archived phases into the root's milestone. Workstreams still share the project's research/. (#5858) (#6288)
  • The command-exit-zero gate message now keeps the end of a failing command's output — the error that lands last in stderr/stdout is no longer cut off, and the command exited N: / command timed out after Ns: prefix still counts toward the 2000-character cap; whitespace-only stderr no longer hides stdout on a non-zero exit or the command head on a timeout. (#5593) (#5639)
  • A quoted grep pattern that ends in a regex escape like \.env is no longer denied as a secret-file read — the secret-read guard read the backslash of the escape as a path separator in Bash operands, so grep -E 'migrations/|\.env' was blocked although no file named .env is read. Windows-shaped paths (C:\proj\.env, \\host\share\.env) are still blocked. (#5883) (#6244)
  • The secret-read guard no longer denies a grep pattern, an exclusion value or a jq program that merely names a secret file — grep -n 'packages/db/.env.local' src/setup.ts, grep -rn token . --exclude=.env and jq '.env.X' settings.json were blocked although the command opens none of those names. Every file the command does open, a pattern file (-f), --exclude-from, and any command with an option the guard does not know stay checked. (#5884) (#6247)

Security

  • Shell quoting could hide a secret read from the secret-read guard — echo $'x\'y'; cat .env and cat $'.e\x6ev' are now blocked. The guard reads $'…' under bash and zsh, and as plain quotes under dash, judges a word by its decoded value, and closes four shapes it allowed: a brace as a heredoc tag, a quote in zsh's arithmetic, $[ ] inside ${…}, and a line continuation inside ${…} under dash. The scan from before this change still runs on every command, and a differential test over 30,000 generated commands found none it blocked that is now allowed. A command it cannot finish scanning is blocked as command-too-complex. (#5255) (#5285)

Full Changelog: https://github.com/open-gsd/gsd-core/compare/v1.21.0...v1.22.0

hugohe3/ppt-master ↗ 1 release · 1 stable
v6.10.0 Stable2026-11-19T03:26:22Z

Personal templates created through Create Template now live in a Git-ignored local library so repository updates never trip over personal registrations, the OpenAI image backend defaults to gpt-image-2.6-sunburst, Volcengine adds Seedream 5.0 with Ark as the default gateway, and a set of conversion, icon, notes, and Edit Native round-trip fixes resolves silent content loss and export aborts.

Templates and repository updates

  • Personal library workspaces and indexes now default to Git-ignored skills/ppt-master/templates/local/<kind_dir>/ while official built-in templates stay read-only unless --builtin is passed to register_template.py. Confirm UI, chat discovery, apply_template.py, and mirror_template_materialize.py merge built-in and local layers in memory without mutating tracked files (fd2f058d, 7e0df670).
  • update_repo.py automatically migrates additive unstaged personal entries out of tracked *_index.json files into templates/local/ before git pull --ff-only, so existing clones with registered custom templates update cleanly (fd2f058d).

AI image generation

  • The OpenAI backend defaults to gpt-image-2.6-sunburst across image_gen.py, .env.example, and documentation (5495a5cb).
  • Volcengine supports doubao-seedream-5-0-pro-260628 (new default) and doubao-seedream-5-0-flash-260915 with per-model pixel-budget enforcement and defaults to the Ark endpoint while preserving existing LAS setups (#347, 3c6ffd0e).
  • Image output-path tests run inside temporary directories instead of touching /out (#351, b243a950).

Edit Native PPTX, icons, notes, and source conversion

  • Edit Native PPTX: newly authored block and inline Office Math (a14:m) on round-trip slides whose source XML never declared xmlns:a14 now retain scoped MCE prefix bindings without causing false-positive conflicts on unrelated shape edits (#355 by @DoctrineAlanK, 05007015).
  • Icons: tabler-outline icons that combine stroke geometry with filled sub-elements (such as percentage-50, discount, and accessible) retain their filled details and resolve inline style stroke/fill overrides consistently in DrawingML (#354 by @kevindesuyo, ea6b25da).
  • Speaker notes: narration lines starting with # followed immediately by digits or text (such as #1 ...) are no longer mistaken for page headings in total_md_split.py or stripped in exported PowerPoint speaker notes (#352 by @kevindesuyo, 1244d814).
  • Source conversion (source_to_md): HTML pages declaring iso-8859-1 / latin1 aliases are decoded with the WHATWG windows-1252 C1 mapping across both doc_to_md.py and web_to_md.py without affecting non-Latin-1 encodings or plain-text responses (#353 by @kevindesuyo, 98d47ea2), and web_to_md.py writes forward-slash image links on Windows (#349 by @pranav-sirnapalli, e2820d32).

Documentation

  • Added the PPT Master wordmark/logo to both READMEs, updated recommended driving-model guidance to Kimi K3.1 and Gemini, and corrected the skill-only release ZIP size figure (01fe5493, 8b1c8131, 00981ba3, 3af3e8ef, 14810331, d8ca696b).

can1357/oh-my-pi ↗ 1 release · 1 stable
v19.0.0 Stable2026-11-19T00:02:14Z

@oh-my-pi/pi-agent-core

Added

  • Added support for excluding custom messages from model context and compaction or branch summaries, while allowing queued steering and follow-up messages to continue correctly after excluded messages.

@oh-my-pi/pi-ai

Added

  • Added a prewarm stream option for Anthropic Messages requests to refresh prompt caches without generating billable output; it is rejected for budget thinking, forced tool choice, and structured output.
  • Claude usage reports now include the account subscription plan in metadata.subscriptionPlan, supporting Max 5x, Max 20x, Pro, Team, and Enterprise plans.
  • Added retryUnreachableBroker and onBrokerRetry options to discoverAuthStorage, allowing hosts to control startup retries for temporarily unavailable authentication brokers and display retry status.

Fixed

  • Fixed Claude account failover after a shared weekly usage limit recovers, ensuring accounts become available again when usage reporting no longer shows that window.
  • Authentication broker startup now tolerates temporary broker restarts and connection interruptions by retrying for up to 30 seconds, while still failing immediately for rejected tokens or malformed snapshots.
  • Improved authentication broker compatibility across client and broker versions by tolerating unknown credential, usage, and observed-usage fields and values.
  • Fixed Codex Spark usage meters so reports that identify the meter only by metered_feature remain correctly scoped to the Spark model.
  • Fixed Antigravity CLI requests for models whose available IDs include effort suffixes, such as resolving gemini-4-hydrogen when only gemini-4-hydrogen-high is offered.

@oh-my-pi/pi-catalog

Added

  • Added support for prompt-cache prewarming on the first-party Anthropic endpoint via the isPromptCachePrewarmSupported compatibility capability.

Fixed

  • Fixed hosted judgment requests for TypeSafe, OpenRouter, Command Code, and OpenCode Zen when evaluating more than 32 questions.
  • Improved Devin image handling to support more conversation images within provider limits, and updated image compaction for Devin Gemini models to reflect their billed token usage.
  • Fixed Devin Gemini model pricing so models switch from promotional to list pricing when promotions end.
  • Fixed Kiro API key authentication (KIRO_API_KEY and ksk_…) so model listing, chat, and /usage requests work correctly.

@oh-my-pi/pi-coding-agent

Breaking Changes

  • Changed SDK cache warming controls: createAgentSession.cacheWarming now accepts the mode cap "off", "streaming", or "idle"; the lower of this value and providers.cacheWarming applies. Use "off" instead of false.
  • Replaced artifact://N links with readable, listable, and completable local://N.log files at the session's local:// root. artifact:// links in existing sessions are not migrated.
  • Replaced session.handoff(focus) with session.compact(focus, { mode: "handoff" }); use abortCompaction() to cancel it.
  • Consolidated shared background processes under one per-user daemon rather than project-specific brokers. Daemons and services from older versions are not adopted; omp ps now reports global, project, and session units together, removes --global, changes --json output to {daemon, reports}, and no longer adds ?project= to dsm:// handles.
  • Renamed SDK deferSkillDescriptionCompression to deferHintCompression and startSkillDescriptionCompression() to startHintCompression(); these settings and methods now also cover MCP namespace hints.

Added

  • Added built-in MCP transforms, including the Codex Desktop node_repl replacement. Built-ins can be disabled with mcp.builtinTransforms; /mcp list identifies replaced servers and /mcp enable node_repl can explicitly connect one.
  • Added optional MCP eval namespaces through mcp.evalNamespaces, exposing servers as JavaScript and Python namespaces with documentation at xd://eval/<server>. Results preserve text and structured data, and binary resources are saved as local:// files.
  • Added model-generated names for /tan agents and their tray cards, with a readable random fallback when naming fails or times out.
  • Added configurable cache-warming strategies (auto, keepalive, and compact), including long-tool-call keepalive and pre-expiry prompt compaction. Cache-warming events now report the selected method and costs.
  • Added optional cross-instance task discovery and messaging with task.crossInstance, exposing same-folder instances through agent://peers.
  • Added btw mode: drafts beginning with ? submit as /btw questions and receive distinct UI indication.
  • Added Tern live cards for running subagents and background jobs, including progress, tool counts, status, controls, transcripts, and restored access to finished agents.
  • Added daemon update survival and optional TCP hosting through OMP_DAEMON_TCP, preserving running services, PTYs, and language servers across daemon upgrades.
  • Loaded speech-to-text and text-to-speech models once per machine, unloading them after 15 minutes of inactivity.
  • Added ovnfs worktree support to /wt, the project resume picker, and omp worktree list/clear, while preserving session resume from the source repository after checkout removal.
  • Added Claude subscription plans to omp usage and /usage output.
  • Added progress reporting for background skill-description and MCP namespace hint generation.
  • Added excludeFromContext to pi.sendMessage payloads so extensions can display and save per-turn messages without sending them to the model.
  • Added a 30-second retry window for CLI and print-mode commands while the authentication broker restarts or is briefly unavailable.
  • Added a notify option to the bash tool for reporting matching output from background commands and named services, waking idle sessions or ending blocked waits.

Changed

  • Improved cache warming for Anthropic-compatible providers with no-output prewarming where supported and billing based on actual replay costs; task subagents now keep prompt caches warm during long tool calls when streaming warming is enabled.
  • Generated skill descriptions and MCP namespace hints once per machine, sharing results and progress across omp windows.
  • Preserved thinking levels when switching models, adjusting only when the selected model cannot support the current level; model defaults apply only when no level is set.
  • Extended repeated reply and subagent waits to use the session's prompt-cache lifetime, and made subagent waits follow the live parent's next message when appropriate.
  • Made /handoff run as background compaction while preserving the current response and newly arriving messages.
  • Redesigned ask prompts as a single scrolling form above the composer, preserving partial answers and allowing users to skip pending questions by sending a message.
  • Enabled composer predictions by default, showing the active model's suggested next message after completed turns; disable them in Settings → Interaction.
  • Disabled tool-call intent tracing by default in print, RPC, ACP, and SDK sessions; enable it with tools.intentTracing or PI_INTENT_TRACING.
  • Updated ovnfs checkout guidance and git guard messages to explain how to land, pull, or discard changes.
  • Improved subagent completion probes and side requests so they focus on deliverables and do not include still-streaming reasoning as plain history.
  • Standardized wait behavior to a 5-second initial idle wait followed by four-minute back-to-back waits.
  • Always provide subagents with the shared LSP tool; task.enableLsp now controls only LSP formatting and diagnostics for edits.
  • Improved bash services, PTYs, and embedded shells with session environment inheritance, session-file URL support, clearer readiness validation, and more transparent minimized output.
  • Named managed Chromium clearly when running in a window and improved browser, screenshot, upload, frame, selector, network, and CDP behavior across tabs and browser backends.
  • Improved hashline editing with verbatim replacement bodies, safer range and path validation, clearer parse and syntax diagnostics, support for larger files and SSH files, and more predictable raw reads and line selectors.
  • Improved MCP reliability with ten-minute tool-call deadlines, longer deadlines when progress is reported, clearer timeout and connection errors, and one retry after expired HTTP sessions.
  • Expanded the embedded shell with portable built-ins and internal-URL support for common workflows, including file inspection, archiving, process control, environment loading, file watching, locking, speech, opening files, and patch application.
  • Improved LSP and debugging diagnostics, language coverage, workspace detection, references, rename, code actions, and server lifecycle reporting.
  • Improved eval tooling with Python 3.9+ validation, UTF-8 execution, package installation refreshes, full tool results, protocol-file access, threaded Python helpers, and clearer kernel-loss and timeout messages.
  • Improved commit drafts and summaries to handle hooks, amend operations, worktrees, filters, line endings, large untracked trees, review files, and JavaScript/Python API parity more accurately.
  • Improved URL, archive, GitHub, Terraform Registry, package documentation, web search, and remote-file handling, including filtering, pagination, binary conversion, archive member access, and clearer provider errors.
  • Improved usage and account policy reporting so subscription limits and scoped exhaustion identify the affected plan or meter accurately.
  • Improved worktree, process, wait, agent messaging, session restore, memory retention, Spotlight, Windows shell, and macOS performance behavior.

Fixed

  • Fixed MCP resource links and binary resources so they remain accessible as readable files or inline content rather than being dropped or shown only as placeholders.
  • Fixed commit drafts after pre-commit hooks and partial prior commits so the index, diff, selected blocks, amended parent, review files, and generated summaries remain accurate.
  • Fixed automatic Hindsight and Mnemopi retention during runs with async work, subagents, or peer messages, preventing duplicate or overlapping retention.
  • Fixed blocked agents and waits by reporting waiters and dependency chains, delivering messages to queued agents, and correctly handling killed, evicted, resumed, or empty-output jobs.
  • Fixed session resume and process-result handling across unified-daemon upgrades.
  • Fixed thinking-level display and model-switcher behavior for subagents, auto-thinking sessions, and manually adjusted levels.
  • Fixed worktree cloning, ovnfs/Git transitions, service readiness checks, shell environment inheritance, Windows junction removal, process substitution, checksums, and background command cancellation.
  • Fixed browser automation reliability across screenshots, clicks, fills, tabs, frames, dialogs, uploads, CDP endpoints, stale connections, concurrent evaluations, browser startup, viewport emulation, clipboard, and platform-specific input.
  • Fixed hashline, AST editing, generated-file detection, numbered content, remote-file editing, syntax diagnostics, path handling, and line-ending behavior.
  • Fixed eval kernels, Python and JavaScript helpers, package imports, nested tool calls, internal URLs, and moved working directories.
  • Fixed LSP, language-server startup, diagnostics, references, rename, code actions, project loading, file types, and debugger adapter selection.
  • Fixed command-output summarization so relevant context, metadata, Git output, test timing, AWS output, lint diagnostics, and binary detection are preserved instead of being misclassified.
  • Fixed read, grep, glob, URL, archive, SQLite, video, RSS, GitHub, package-documentation, SSH, and web-search handling across selectors, filters, pagination, archives, binary content, and provider failures.
  • Fixed todo operations, ask compatibility and timeout behavior, image generation validation, GitHub PR pushes, plan proposals, and session accounting for side-channel model calls.
  • Fixed MCP OAuth reauthentication errors to expose registration endpoints, status details, and remedies, and fixed auth-broker shutdown so refreshed tokens are saved safely.
  • Fixed Spotlight retry and account handling, including waitlisted accounts, rate limits, outages, and request identification.
  • Fixed cross-platform shell and desktop behavior, including macOS startup and redraw performance, Windows file operations and application lookup, speech output, and desktop opening.

@oh-my-pi/collab-web

Fixed

  • Fixed web search cards without a synthesized answer displaying raw numbered result lists; they now show a concise excerpt for each source instead.

@oh-my-pi/pi-natives

Added

  • Added VcsGitRepo.worktreeToGit() to convert worktree content into the blob representation produced by git add.
  • Added support for using a Shell as a process's shell, routing commands through the process's standard streams while preserving the process session and terminal for external commands.
  • Added ShellRunOptions.speech to connect the host speech handler to the shell's say command.
  • Added an in-process file shell builtin with broad file-type detection, MIME and encoding output, archive/image/document support, and compatibility with the commonly used file command options.

Changed

  • Improved typing and key-press responsiveness in iPhone Mirroring, reducing per-character latency by approximately half.

Fixed

  • Fixed screenshots of partially off-screen Linux windows on Wayland and X11 so the visible region is captured and coordinate-based zoom targets correctly.
  • Fixed Wayland input operations on desktops without an available input route to fail immediately with a clear PermissionDenied error instead of attempting unnecessary screen capture first.
  • Fixed macOS computer interactions so background clicks, key presses, pop-up menu actions, and takeover calls restore the previously active window within the call.
  • Fixed macOS pop-up menu reads immediately after typing so they reflect the option selected by the entered letters.

@oh-my-pi/omp-stats

Breaking Changes

  • Spotlight recovery counts reads from local spill files toward recovery counts; reads from artifact references in older sessions continue to count as before.

Added

  • Expanded the Overview's Token mix to include off-transcript model calls—such as title generation, job captions, judge queries, and cache warming—with per-source request counts, token totals, and usage shares.
  • Added a Decision models card to the Overview showing requests, tokens, and usage share by judgment use case for native judgment models.
  • Request details for title-generation and judge calls now display the generated reply or recorded judge answers.

Changed

  • Token mix shares for the main agent, subagents, and advisor now reflect conversation turns only, excluding side-channel model calls.

Fixed

  • Hardened the dashboard against DNS rebinding by rejecting requests made through unconfigured public host names. IP addresses, localhost, LAN short names, local network domains, and Tailscale names remain supported; configure reverse-proxy or other custom domains with OMP_STATS_ALLOWED_HOSTS, including wildcard subdomains such as *.example.com.

@oh-my-pi/pi-tui

Breaking Changes

  • Full-output references and the [raw output: …] footer now use local://N.log instead of artifact://N.

Added

  • Added the btw composer mode, including the btwMode theme color in all bundled themes.
  • Added feature-gated native docking for runtime children, allowing components to appear only on terminals that advertise the required capability.
  • Added options to preserve intraword underscores when stripping inline Markdown and to open JobsSheet on a selected job.
  • Custom message payloads can now be excluded from model context while remaining available in normalized and restored saved entries.
  • Added transcript blocks for output reported by running background jobs and services.

Changed

  • Reworked ask dialogs into a single stacked flow: the active question stays open while answered questions fold into compact summaries with their selected options visible. This applies across terminal and native interfaces and improves keyboard navigation, sizing, and host composer integration.
  • Improved composer behavior while an ask dialog is pending: the composer indicates that typing there will skip the question, folds to a compact state when appropriate, and offers an explicit Skip & send action for drafts that bypass the ask.
  • Added support for folded and faint composer states and clearer visual indication when the interface is awaiting an answer.
  • Model search now supports provider/model queries, prioritizing exact provider matches and searching that provider's model IDs first.

Fixed

  • Improved background-job notices in Tern by grouping all output under one header and showing the complete lines.
  • Fixed Tern composer handling of -> and => so typing them no longer unexpectedly inserts a line break.
  • Fixed web search results without a synthesized answer to show each source once with a concise cleaned excerpt.
  • Fixed long Tern tool-approval prompts so their details scroll within the available window and the choices and Approve/Deny controls remain visible.
  • Fixed table chart unit display and change-factor calculations for measurements with scaled units.
  • Fixed checkbox groups so pressing Enter with nothing selected first checks the focused box.
  • Ask forms now open each question with its preselected answer, allowing Enter to accept the recommendation without replacing it.

@oh-my-pi/pi-utils

Added

  • Added utilities for formatting and parsing session spill URLs using the local://N.log format.
  • Added postmortem.exitCleanlyOnStopSignals() so long-running services exit successfully after completing cleanup on SIGINT or SIGTERM.

Fixed

  • Fixed Windows shell discovery incorrectly selecting the WSL bash.exe launcher, which could cause services to run under WSL.
  • Fixed archive opening so the extraction-size limit is enforced when an oversized member is read rather than preventing access to the entire archive.
  • Fixed OMP_LOG_LEVEL so it also controls console output from services such as omp auth-broker serve, not just log-file output.
  • Fixed Markdown parsing so emphasis and link labels are not prematurely terminated by marker characters inside code spans, autolinks, inline HTML tags, or link destinations.

@oh-my-pi/pi-wire

Added

  • Added tray state and interaction properties, including focus, peek, quiet, main status, item references, card status and actions, and event state.
  • Added layout transition properties to nodes for coordinating layout animations.
  • Added support for folding a dock composer into its compact row.

Automattic/harper ↗ 1 release · 1 stable
v3.3.0 Stable2026-11-18T23:27:37Z

What's Changed

Another release! This one isn't quite a big or heavy on the bugfixes as some of our other releases, but I wanted to get something out to you all anyway.

Most significantly, @ecairol did some amazing work enhancing how Harper ranks its spelling suggestions. You'll find that the suggestions in this version are for more correct in context than ever before. Definitely a welcome improvement. We also added the ability to load Weirpacks in the Desktop app, after several requests. In the Chrome Extension, you should see more relevant checks in more places.

Hopefully we can get another release out soon with some more improvements we've been cooking up. Until then, cheers!

The PRs We Merged
